## Phase: P-444 — New Package: `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation` — FluentValidation Rule Adapter <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P444 -->

> A new, **ninth `01.Core` package**, `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation`, referencing `SharedKernel.Validation` (P-443) and the third-party `FluentValidation` NuGet package. A thin `IRuleBuilder<T, string>` extension-method adapter (`ValidationRuleBuilderExtensions`) exposing `.MustBeValidIban()`, `.MustBeValidBic()`, `.MustBeValidPan()`, `.MustBeValidCurrencyCode()`, `.MustBeValidCountryCode()`, `.MustBeValidPhoneNumber()`, `.MustBeValidVatNumber()`, and `.MustBeValidNationalId(Func<T, string> countryCodeSelector, INationalIdValidatorRegistry registry)` — every rule delegates to the matching `SharedKernel.Validation` static validator's `IsValid`/`Validate` and, on failure, sets the FluentValidation error code to the matching `ValidationErrorCodes` constant so a failure surfaces the identical code whether reached via the standalone `SharedKernel.Validation` call or through this FluentValidation adapter.
> Keeping `FluentValidation` out of `SharedKernel.Validation` itself (P-443) means a service that only wants the standalone `Result`/`Guard` surface — a Temporal activity, a lightweight worker with no MediatR pipeline — never pulls in FluentValidation transitively. This mirrors the platform's existing "keep the core dependency-free, split the framework adapter" instinct already applied elsewhere (e.g. `SharedKernel.Cryptography` staying ASP.NET/JWT/OIDC-free while `12.Security.Oidc` depends on it, never the reverse).
> **Shipped end to end.** Design-locked in a prior pass (D-48, marked `●`); Scaffold/Core/Tests/Docs/Published (S-21, C-61, T-50, DO-26, P-20→P-22) implemented and shipped in this pass, all marked `●`. `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation` is the platform's ninth published `01.Core` package: 26/26 `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation.Tests` passing, 56/56 `SharedKernel.Consumer.Tests` passing (extended from 54/54 with 2 new consumer-verification tests against the packed NuGet package), packed to `./nupkgs` at `1.0.0-alpha.0.794` (matching every sibling package's MinVer-derived version, `SharedKernel.Validation` repacked at the same version). Rules are built on FluentValidation's `Custom(...)` extension (not `Must(...)`+`.WithErrorCode(...)`) so `ValidationFailure.ErrorCode` always carries the *exact* `ValidationErrorCodes` constant the underlying validator produced — verified concretely against `IbanValidator`, whose `Validate` can fail with three distinct codes. The public return type is FluentValidation 11.x's real `IRuleBuilderOptionsConditions<T,string>` (a CS0266 compiler error caught the phase-spec's `IRuleBuilder<T,string>` shorthand describing the input-parameter type, not the fluent-chaining return type). **Source correction:** `05.Application.Behaviors.Validation.ValidationBehavior` today projects `Error.Validation(failure.PropertyName, failure.ErrorMessage)` — the FluentValidation property name becomes the downstream `Error.Code`, NOT `failure.ErrorCode` as this phase's own narrative and this session's first XML-doc draft both assumed; corrected in the XML docs and both READMEs before shipping. T-50's `ValidationBehavior` interop test is a locally-written harness reproducing that real aggregation shape (read from source) rather than a live cross-domain reference, per this phase's own constraint.
> **Depends on P-443** — this package cannot be implemented before `SharedKernel.Validation`'s static validators/`ValidationErrorCodes` exist. **P-443 shipped in the immediately preceding session**, so this dependency was satisfied.
> WO-067.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |

| D-48 | Define `ValidationRuleBuilderExtensions` — the full `IRuleBuilder<T, string>.MustBeValid*()` extension-method surface listed above, each mapping 1:1 to a `SharedKernel.Validation` static validator and the matching `ValidationErrorCodes` constant; XML docs include a documented recipe showing composition with `05.Application.Behaviors`'s existing `ValidationBehavior` (a service's `AbstractValidator<T>` calling `.MustBeValidIban()` inside a rule already wired into that pipeline behavior, no extra plumbing needed) | SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation | `●` |
| S-21 | Create `01.Core/SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation/SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation.csproj` targeting `net10.0`; add project reference to `SharedKernel.Validation` and NuGet reference to `FluentValidation`; create nested `.Tests.csproj` with xUnit reference; register both in `Platform.SharedKernel.slnx` under solution folder `01.Core` | SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation | `●` |
| C-61 | Implement `ValidationRuleBuilderExtensions` exactly per the D-48 design | SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation | `●` |
| T-50 | Unit: each `.MustBeValid*()` rule — valid input passes FluentValidation, invalid input fails with the matching `ValidationErrorCodes` error code attached; an end-to-end `AbstractValidator<T>` composition test proving interop with `05.Application.Behaviors`'s `ValidationBehavior` shape (constructed directly here, not a live cross-domain reference — `16.Testing`/`05.Application` remain out of this package's dependency graph) | SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation.Tests | `●` |

## Phase: P-446 — Async-Capable `IEncryptionKeyProvider` + Envelope Encryption + Key-Caching Seam (BREAKING) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P446 -->

> **Source correction carried forward from the WO-068 dispatch input:** `IEncryptionKeyProvider` already lives in `01.Core/SharedKernel.Cryptography/Symmetric/IEncryptionKeyProvider.cs` — it is not being extracted from `06.Persistence.EfCore`, it is already the shared seam `06.Persistence.EfCore`, `15.Integration.Webhooks`, `16.Testing`, and `17.Workflows.Temporal` all consume. The real, narrow gap: `GetCurrentKey()`/`GetKey(string)` are synchronous, structurally incompatible with a genuine network-bound KMS/HSM call (Azure Key Vault, AWS KMS, HashiCorp Vault) without a blocking-on-async anti-pattern.
> **Breaking change to `IEncryptionKeyProvider`:** its two members become `GetCurrentKeyAsync(CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<CryptographicKey>` and `GetKeyAsync(string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<CryptographicKey?>` — the synchronous members are removed outright, not kept as an additive overload (a KMS-backed implementer must not be allowed to silently offer a synchronous, thread-blocking path). Every implementer must migrate.
> **`ISymmetricEncryptionService` gets new async overloads, additively** (`EncryptAsync`/`DecryptAsync`/`EncryptToStringAsync`/`DecryptToStringAsync`, all `CancellationToken`-aware). The existing synchronous `Encrypt`/`Decrypt`/`EncryptToString`/`DecryptToString` members are **retained**, not removed, and internally bridge to the now-async key provider via `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()` on the returned `ValueTask` — safe and genuinely non-blocking when the registered `IEncryptionKeyProvider` resolves synchronously (the config-based default, and `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` on a cache hit), but a real thread-blocking call when a registered provider is genuinely network-bound (a raw KMS call on a cache miss). XML docs on the sync overloads must say this in capitals and direct hot-path/high-throughput callers to the new `*Async` overloads instead. This resolves the ripple that a fully-async `ISymmetricEncryptionService` would otherwise force onto five downstream domains (`02.Caching`, `06.Persistence`, `07.Messaging`, `15.Integration`, `17.Workflows`) — most acutely `06.Persistence`'s EF Core `ValueConverter` pipeline, which is **structurally synchronous-only** and cannot be made to await anything; that specific problem is `06.Persistence`'s own follow-on phase (P-448, out of this domain's jurisdiction), not solved here, but this design decision is what keeps it solvable there at all rather than forcing a platform-wide breaking cascade from this one phase.
> **New additive envelope-encryption contract**, `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` (`GenerateDataKeyAsync` — ask the KMS-held master key to generate+wrap a fresh symmetric data key, returning both the plaintext data key for immediate local use and its wrapped form safe to persist; `UnwrapDataKeyAsync` — ask the KMS to unwrap a previously-wrapped data key by master-key id, so master key material never leaves the KMS boundary) — distinct from and non-breaking to `IEncryptionKeyProvider`'s direct-retrieval consumers. A provider (e.g. P-447's Azure Key Vault one) may implement both interfaces.
> **New additive caching decorator**, `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` (wraps any `IEncryptionKeyProvider`, bounded-TTL, `TimeProvider`-driven, single-flight-per-key refresh to avoid a cache-stampede thundering herd on expiry — never a stale-serve-while-revalidating pattern; an expired entry always re-fetches from the inner provider before any caller proceeds). Ships with **no package-owned DI extension** (mirrors the `IIdGenerator`/`SystemClock(TimeProvider)` precedent) — a consuming service composes it explicitly: `services.AddSingleton<IEncryptionKeyProvider>(sp => new CachedEncryptionKeyProvider(new MyKmsProvider(...), TimeProvider.System, ttl))`.
> **Fail-closed is structural, not a documented convention**: an unreachable KMS during `GetCurrentKeyAsync`/`GetKeyAsync` (whether called directly, through `EncryptAsync`/`DecryptAsync`, or through the sync bridge) propagates as a thrown exception — this contract does not adopt `Result<T>` (unchanged from today's shape) and there is no code path that silently proceeds with a placeholder/no-op key. `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` never serves an entry past its TTL bound under any condition, including a concurrent inner-provider failure during refresh — a failed refresh propagates the exception to every caller waiting on that single-flight resolution, it never falls back to the expired cached value.
> **In-jurisdiction migration**: the one `IEncryptionKeyProvider` implementer inside `01.Core`'s own boundary — the test double used by `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests` itself — is migrated in this same phase (C-65/T-53). **Out of jurisdiction, flagged for the owning domain's own planner**: `06.Persistence.EfCore`'s `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider`/`NullEncryptionKeyProvider` migration (06.Persistence's P-448, where the EF Core `ValueConverter` synchronous-pipeline problem is the central design question), `16.Testing`'s `FakeEncryptionKeyProvider` migration (16.Testing's P-450), and `15.Integration.Webhooks`'s own provider usage (15.Integration's own follow-up) are **not** tracked as tasks in this file.
> Design-locked in this pass (D-49→D-52, marked `●`); Core/Tests/Docs (C-62→C-65, T-51→T-53, DO-27) and the breaking repack (P-23) remain `○` pending a future implementation pass.
> WO-068.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|

| D-50 | Design `ISymmetricEncryptionService`'s additive async surface — `EncryptAsync(byte[] plaintext, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<EncryptedPayload>`, `DecryptAsync(EncryptedPayload payload, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<Result<byte[]>>`, `EncryptToStringAsync`/`DecryptToStringAsync` mirrors; existing synchronous members retained, internally bridging via `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()` on the async key-provider call — XML docs on every sync member must state IN CAPITALS that this blocks a real thread when the registered `IEncryptionKeyProvider` is genuinely network-bound, and direct high-throughput/hot-path callers to the `*Async` overloads | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-51 | Design the additive `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` contract — `GenerateDataKeyAsync(CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<EnvelopeDataKey>`, `UnwrapDataKeyAsync(byte[] wrappedDataKey, string masterKeyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<Result<byte[]>>`; define `EnvelopeDataKey` sealed record (`.PlaintextKey → byte[]` — use immediately, never persist; `.WrappedKey → byte[]` — persist this; `.MasterKeyId → string`); a provider may implement both `IEncryptionKeyProvider` and `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-52 | Design `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` (sealed, implements `IEncryptionKeyProvider`, decorator pattern — constructor takes an inner `IEncryptionKeyProvider`, a `TimeProvider`, and a `TimeSpan ttl`) — bounded-TTL, single-flight-per-key refresh (no cache-stampede thundering herd, no stale-serve-while-revalidating), never serves past TTL, propagates an inner-provider failure directly to every caller awaiting that refresh; no package-owned DI extension — plain composition at the consumer's composition root, mirroring the `IIdGenerator`/`SystemClock(TimeProvider)` no-extension precedent | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

## Phase: P-447 — New Package: `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` — Azure Key Vault Keys Provider <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P447 -->

> A new, **tenth `01.Core` package**, `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`, implementing the now-async `IEncryptionKeyProvider` and the new `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` (P-446) against Azure Key Vault Keys (`Azure.Security.KeyVault.Keys` + `Azure.Identity` for credential resolution). A genuine vendor-SDK package, kept out of the zero-third-party-dependency `SharedKernel.Cryptography` core — the same reasoning that puts `SharedKernel.Storage.S3`/`.Obs` in their own packages rather than inside a dependency-free abstractions core, and explicitly **not** the same case as WO-034's declined RSA/ECDSA split (that was one BCL provider selectable by keyed DI; this is a genuine external vendor system reached over the network).
> **Design resolution for "implements both envelope-wrap and direct-retrieval modes" (a real tension, recorded honestly rather than glossed over):** Azure Key Vault Keys does not export raw HSM-protected key material by default — the vendor-idiomatic operation is `CryptographyClient.WrapKeyAsync`/`UnwrapKeyAsync` (RSA-OAEP or AES-KW depending on key type), which is exactly `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider`'s shape. `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s `IEncryptionKeyProvider` (direct-retrieval) implementation is therefore built **on top of** its own `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` implementation internally, not as two independent code paths: `GetCurrentKeyAsync` generates (or returns a process-lifetime-cached) local AES-256 data key via `GenerateDataKeyAsync`, exposing only the already-in-memory plaintext data key as `CryptographicKey.Material` — the vault's own master key material never crosses the process boundary either way. This satisfies the letter of "implements both modes" via one coherent internal mechanism rather than a second, parallel raw-export code path that Azure Key Vault Keys does not sanely support in the general (HSM-backed) case. This resolution is recorded here as a design decision, not left implicit for the implementer to improvise.
> **Fail-closed**: any Azure SDK exception (unreachable vault, `RequestFailedException` for permission/auth failure) during `GetCurrentKeyAsync`/`GetKeyAsync`/`GenerateDataKeyAsync`/`UnwrapDataKeyAsync` propagates directly — no silent fallback, matching P-446's structural fail-closed contract exactly.
> **Composes with, never duplicates, P-446's caching decorator**: this package ships zero caching of its own — a consuming service wraps `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` in `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` explicitly if it wants bounded-TTL caching, avoiding two independent caches with different TTL semantics colliding.
> **Depends on P-446** — cannot be implemented before the async `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` contracts exist.
> Distinct from `13.ServiceDefaults`'s existing `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultConfiguration()` (P-398) — that wires Azure Key Vault as an `IConfiguration` source; this is a key-management contract implementation. The two are unrelated and never reference each other.
> Design-locked in this pass (D-53, marked `●`); Scaffold/Core/Tests/Docs/Published (S-22, C-66/C-67, T-54, DO-28, P-24→P-26) remain `○` pending a future implementation pass.
> WO-068.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-53 | Define `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` (sealed, implements `IEncryptionKeyProvider` + `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` — direct-retrieval built atop the envelope path internally, per the design resolution above); `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions` (bound via `IOptions<T>`, validated via `AddValidatedOptions` — `.VaultUri`, a `KeyName`-per-`keyId` map, and a pluggable `TokenCredential` factory defaulting to `Azure.Identity.DefaultAzureCredential`); `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography(IConfiguration configuration)` DI extension registering the options and the provider as both `IEncryptionKeyProvider` and `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` (same singleton instance, two service-type registrations) — no caching registered by default | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| S-22 | Create `01.Core/SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure/SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure.csproj` targeting `net10.0`; add project references to `SharedKernel.Cryptography` + `SharedKernel.Configuration`, NuGet references to `Azure.Security.KeyVault.Keys` + `Azure.Identity`; create nested `.Tests.csproj`; register both in `Platform.SharedKernel.slnx` under solution folder `01.Core` | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| C-66 | Implement `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` exactly per the D-53 design and its internal envelope-atop-direct-retrieval resolution | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| C-67 | Implement `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions` validation and `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` DI extension exactly per D-53 | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |

## Phase: P-451 — RFC 6238 TOTP / RFC 4226 HOTP Generation, Verification, and Replay Guard <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P451 -->

> An additive extension to the existing `SharedKernel.Cryptography` package (no new package — a pure cryptographic primitive with no identity-stack dependency, the same reasoning that already keeps password hashing and AES-GCM here rather than in `12.Security`). Adds: `Base32` codec (RFC 4648, unpadded — the encoding authenticator apps expect); `IHotpGenerator`/`HotpGenerator` (RFC 4226 core — HMAC-SHA1 default per the RFC, SHA-256/SHA-512 opt-in per common TOTP-era extensions, dynamic truncation per RFC 4226 §5.3); `ITotpGenerator`/`TotpGenerator` (RFC 6238 — composes `IHotpGenerator` internally with a time-derived counter, `IClock`-sourced "now" when no explicit timestamp is supplied — never `DateTime.UtcNow`); a provisioning-URI builder producing the `otpauth://totp/...` Key Uri Format authenticator apps consume for enrollment; `ITotpReplayGuard` (pluggable, mirrors `12.Security.Oidc`'s DPoP replay-check seam — implemented by the consuming service, never a direct `02.Caching` reference from this package) plus `TotpVerifier` (the orchestration type composing `ITotpGenerator.ValidateCode` + `ITotpReplayGuard` so a valid code cannot be accepted twice inside its validity window); and `RecoveryCodeGenerator` (backup codes via `ISecureRandomGenerator` — this type only generates plaintext codes shown once to the user; hashing them at rest via the existing `IOneWayHasher` before persistence is the consuming service's job, exactly like any other secret).
> A repo-wide grep confirmed zero existing TOTP/HOTP/OTP matches anywhere on the platform. `12.Security` shipped the surface to *read and gate on* a second-factor signal in WO-058/WO-062 (`IUserContext.AuthenticationMethods`/`.AuthTime`/`WasAuthenticatedWith`, `[RequireFreshAuthentication]`/`[RequireAuthenticationMethod]`) but nothing can actually *perform* one — this phase closes that half. The replay guard must live alongside the generator/verifier in the same package, never split out, because `TotpVerifier` needs it internally to reject a reused code — splitting it into a separate package would create a circular dependency between the two halves.
> `ITotpGenerator.ValidateCode` itself stays pure/stateless (no replay awareness) — replay protection is composed one layer up in `TotpVerifier`, keeping the generator honest as a testable, side-effect-free RFC implementation and the replay-guard orchestration a separate, independently swappable concern.
> Design-locked in this pass (D-54→D-57, marked `●`); Core/Tests/Docs (C-68→C-72, T-55→T-57, DO-29) and the additive repack (P-27) remain `○` pending a future implementation pass.
> WO-069.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-54 | Define `Base32` static codec — `Encode(byte[] data) → string` (unpadded RFC 4648 Base32), `Decode(string base32Text) → Result<byte[]>` (never throws on malformed input, matching `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt`'s established "expected failure surfaces as `Result`" shape) | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-55 | Define `IHotpGenerator`/`HotpGenerator` — `GenerateCode(byte[] secret, long counter, int digits = 6, HotpAlgorithm algorithm = Sha1) → string`, `ValidateCode(byte[] secret, string code, long counter, int digits = 6, HotpAlgorithm algorithm = Sha1) → bool`; `HotpAlgorithm` enum (`Sha1` default per RFC 4226, `Sha256`, `Sha512`); dynamic truncation per RFC 4226 §5.3, backed by `HMACSHA1`/`HMACSHA256`/`HMACSHA512` | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-56 | Define `ITotpGenerator`/`TotpGenerator` — `GenerateCode(byte[] secret, int digits = 6, int stepSeconds = 30, HotpAlgorithm algorithm = Sha1) → string` (uses `IClock` for "now"), an explicit-timestamp overload for testability, `ValidateCode(byte[] secret, string code, int digits = 6, int stepSeconds = 30, int driftWindow = 1, HotpAlgorithm algorithm = Sha1) → bool` (`driftWindow` = number of steps before/after the current step to accept); composes `IHotpGenerator` internally via `counter = floor(unixSeconds / stepSeconds)`; define the provisioning-URI builder (`TotpProvisioningUri.Build(string issuer, string accountName, byte[] secret, int digits = 6, int stepSeconds = 30, HotpAlgorithm algorithm = Sha1) → Uri`) matching the `otpauth://totp/{Issuer}:{AccountName}?secret=...&issuer=...&digits=...&period=...&algorithm=...` Key Uri Format | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-57 | Define `ITotpReplayGuard` (`HasBeenUsedAsync(string identityKey, string code, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<bool>`, `MarkUsedAsync(string identityKey, string code, TimeSpan validityWindow, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask`, implemented by the consuming service, no default implementation ships); `TotpVerifier` (sealed — composes `ITotpGenerator.ValidateCode` + `ITotpReplayGuard`; `VerifyAsync(string identityKey, byte[] secret, string code, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<bool>` returns `false` on an invalid code OR a previously-used code, calls `MarkUsedAsync` only after a fresh valid code, before returning `true`); `RecoveryCodeGenerator` (`GenerateCodes(int count = 10, int lengthBytes = 5) → IReadOnlyList<string>`, via `ISecureRandomGenerator`; XML docs state hashing at rest via `IOneWayHasher` is the consuming service's responsibility, this type never persists or hashes) | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-68 | Implement `Base32` exactly per D-54 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-69 | Implement `HotpGenerator` exactly per D-55 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-70 | Implement `TotpGenerator` and `TotpProvisioningUri` exactly per D-56 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-71 | Implement `TotpVerifier` exactly per D-57 (contract-only for `ITotpReplayGuard` — no default implementation) | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-72 | Implement `RecoveryCodeGenerator` exactly per D-57; extend `AddSharedKernelCryptography` to additionally register `IHotpGenerator`, `ITotpGenerator`, and `TotpVerifier` as singletons (stateless); `ITotpReplayGuard` is never registered by this package | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| T-55 | Unit: `Base32` round-trip; `HotpGenerator` against RFC 4226 Appendix D's published test vectors (all 10 counter values, SHA-1); `TotpGenerator` against RFC 6238 Appendix B's published test vectors (SHA-1/SHA-256/SHA-512 at the documented timestamps) | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests | `●` |
| T-56 | Unit: `ITotpGenerator.ValidateCode` — configurable clock-drift window accepts a code generated one step before/after "now" (via a `FakeTimeProvider`-backed `IClock`, never real wall-clock sleeping in a test); a code outside the drift window is rejected; `TotpProvisioningUri.Build` output matches the documented Key Uri Format field-for-field | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests | `●` |
| T-57 | Unit: `TotpVerifier` — **a test submitting the same valid code twice**: the first `VerifyAsync` call returns `true` and calls `MarkUsedAsync`; the second call with the identical code returns `false` via `ITotpReplayGuard.HasBeenUsedAsync`, proving the code cannot be accepted twice inside its validity window (the phase's headline acceptance criterion); `RecoveryCodeGenerator` — output count/length correctness and statistical non-repetition, mirroring `ISecureRandomGenerator`'s existing test shape; DI registration sanity for the three newly-registered `AddSharedKernelCryptography` services | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests | `●` |
| DO-29 | XML doc every new type; add a "TOTP/HOTP — Second-Factor Codes" usage section to `01.Core/README.md` showing enrollment (provisioning URI), challenge (`GenerateCode`), and verification (`TotpVerifier.VerifyAsync` with a consumer-supplied `ITotpReplayGuard`) end to end | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| P-27 | Repack `SharedKernel.Cryptography` with a MINOR version bump (additive — `Base32`, `IHotpGenerator`, `ITotpGenerator`, `ITotpReplayGuard`, `TotpVerifier`, `RecoveryCodeGenerator`, three new `AddSharedKernelCryptography` registrations); if P-446's breaking repack (P-23) has not yet shipped, this MINOR bump lands on top of whatever MAJOR version P-23 established; re-run consumer dependency-graph verification | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

## Phase: P-474 — New Package: `SharedKernel.DataPrivacy` — Classification Taxonomy, Masking Helpers, Data-Subject-Request Contract <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P474 -->

> A new, **eleventh `01.Core` package**, `SharedKernel.DataPrivacy`, referencing only `SharedKernel.Primitives` (for `Result<T>`/`Error` on the data-subject-request contract) — zero third-party NuGet dependency, the same single-package reasoning as `SharedKernel.Cryptography`/`.Compression`/`.Guards`. Ships: `DataClassificationAttribute`/`DataClassification` enum (`Public`/`Internal`/`Confidential`/`Restricted`) and `SensitiveDataCategoryAttribute`/`SensitiveDataCategory` enum (`Pii`/`PaymentCard`/`Credential`/`Health`) — pure metadata attributes usable on any type in any layer, including `03.Domain`/`04.Contracts` (both logging-free, dependency-minimal by rule — a pure metadata attribute violates neither rule); `PiiMasking` static class (`Email`/`Phone`/`Pan`/`Suppress` — pure, allocation-minimal, null-safe, deterministic functions, no reflection anywhere in the package); `IDataSubjectRequestHandler` (`ExportDataAsync`/`RequestErasureAsync`, implemented by each consuming service against its own data, no default/reflection-based implementation ships) plus `DataSubjectExportBundle`/`DataSubjectErasureReceipt` sealed records.
> **The classification attributes are metadata only, by hard design constraint, never read via reflection in a production hot path** — their sole sanctioned consumer is `00.Governance`'s new compile-time analyzer (P-476, out of this domain's jurisdiction) and human documentation. This is not a stylistic preference: the platform already bans reflection-based property walks for logging (root `CLAUDE.md`'s "self-supplied loggable-field surface... never a reflection-based property walk" rule) and enforces adjacent rules mechanically — a classification mechanism that required runtime reflection to be useful would directly contradict the rule it exists to support.
> Zero repo-wide matches for any PII/classification contract exist today. Root `CLAUDE.md` already states the *rule* in prose but provides no shared vocabulary for what makes a field sensitive and no reusable masking — every service invents its own.
> **Explicitly out of scope**: a cross-service erasure orchestrator. This package ships the contract each service implements against its own data, never a coordinator that calls every service's handler — that composition is a plausible future `19.Scheduling`/`17.Workflows` job once multiple services have real handlers, and is not designed here.
> **Cross-domain note, non-binding**: `06.Persistence`'s audit trail (`IAuditTrailWriter`, P-456/WO-071) passes opaque, caller-serialized before/after snapshots — a caller handling classified fields should mask via `PiiMasking.*` before serializing for that writer. This is documentation guidance for `06.Persistence`'s own brain to cross-reference, not a dependency `06.Persistence` takes on this package, and not a task tracked here.
> Design-locked in the earlier pass (D-58→D-60, `●`); Scaffold/Core/Tests/Docs/Published (S-23, C-73→C-75, T-58/T-59, DO-30, P-28→P-30) implemented and shipped end to end in this pass — all `●`.
> WO-076.

## Phase: P-482 — New Package: `SharedKernel.Localization` — Culture-Keyed Message Catalog Contract <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P482 -->

> A new, **twelfth `01.Core` package**, `SharedKernel.Localization`, referencing `SharedKernel.Primitives` and the first-party `Microsoft.Extensions.Localization.Abstractions` NuGet package (not a bespoke resx pipeline). Ships `ILocalizationCatalog` (`TryGetString(string code, CultureInfo culture, out string? value) → bool` — keyed on the same `code` string `01.Core.Primitives.Error`'s factories already require on every call; returns `false`/`null` for an unregistered/untranslated lookup, never throws, never returns a blank string); `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog` (dictionary-backed default implementation, keyed by `(code, CultureInfo.Name)`, seeded via constructor or an `AddTranslation(code, culture, value)` builder method — the zero-config default for a service with a handful of translated codes); `StringLocalizerLocalizationCatalog` (wraps a caller-supplied `IStringLocalizerFactory`, resolving `TryGetString` by checking the returned `LocalizedString.ResourceNotFound` — lets a service with full `.resx`-file tooling compose behind the same seam without a second bespoke pipeline).
> **The load-bearing design decision, carried forward exactly as ratified in the WO-078 dispatch input**: localization happens entirely at the `14.Presentation` boundary (`Error.ToProblemDetails()`, P-484, out of this domain's jurisdiction) by looking up `error.Code` against this catalog — `01.Core.Primitives.Error` itself is **unchanged**, no new property, no breaking change to the platform's single most-depended-upon type. The message baked in at the throw site becomes the fallback when no translation is registered or found — matching the platform's established opt-in convention that an un-translated error behaves identically before and after this ships.
> **Naming collision deliberately avoided**: this package's own DI registration extensions are `AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog(...)`/`AddStringLocalizerCatalog<TResource>()` — **never** `AddSharedKernelLocalization()`, which name is reserved for `13.ServiceDefaults`'s culture-resolution *middleware* entry point (P-483, out of this domain's jurisdiction, `UserPreference` claim → tenant `DefaultCulture` → `Accept-Language` header). The two are distinct concerns (a message-lookup contract here vs. request-culture-resolution middleware there) that must never share one ambiguous name across domains.
> Zero repo-wide matches for `IStringLocalizer`/`CultureInfo`-based resource lookup exist today.
> Design-locked in an earlier pass (D-61/D-62); implemented and shipped end to end in this pass (S-24, C-76/C-77, T-60/T-61, DO-31, P-31→P-33, all `●`). Culture-fallback behavior (parent-culture-chain walk down to `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`) was a judgment call the original design left unspecified — documented explicitly in `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog`'s own XML docs and README section, and covered by dedicated tests; it is specific to that implementation, not mandated by the `ILocalizationCatalog` interface itself.
> WO-078.

| D-62 | Define `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog` (sealed, dictionary-backed, keyed by `(code, CultureInfo.Name)`, constructor-seeded plus a chained `AddTranslation(string code, CultureInfo culture, string value)` builder method) and `StringLocalizerLocalizationCatalog` (sealed, wraps a caller-supplied `IStringLocalizerFactory` + a resource type/name, resolving via `LocalizedString.ResourceNotFound`); define `LocalizationServiceCollectionExtensions.AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog(...)` and `.AddStringLocalizerCatalog<TResource>()` — two independent, mutually-exclusive opt-in registrations, deliberately never named `AddSharedKernelLocalization()` | SharedKernel.Localization | `●` |

## Phase: LoggingEventIdRanges — New Domain Base Constants for 18.Idempotency / 19.Scheduling / 20.Reporting <!-- phase-key: SK.01.LoggingRangesNewDomains -->

> A pure additive extension to the existing `LoggingEventIdRanges` registry (`SharedKernel.Primitives/Logging/LoggingEventIdRanges.cs`, P-249/WO-041): three new `public const int` domain-base fields — `Idempotency = 18000`, `Scheduling = 19000`, `Reporting = 20000` — reserving the next three 1000-wide `EventId` blocks for the three capability domains newly ratified this session (`18.Idempotency`, `19.Scheduling`, `20.Reporting`), each computed identically to every existing field (`{two-digit folder-map number} * 1000`), matching the root `CLAUDE.md` folder map exactly.
> **Cross-cutting housekeeping item, not part of any of the four work orders (WO-067/068/069/076/078) this batch otherwise covers** — flagged directly by the dispatching `arch-lead` input as something this domain must handle because `LoggingEventIdRanges` is `01.Core`-owned and none of the three new domains can author their first `[LoggerMessage]` method without a reserved base. No motivation paragraph beyond "unblock the three new domains" is needed — this mirrors P-249's own original design exactly, just extending its already-established table by three rows.
> Zero breaking impact — no existing domain's base value changes, per `LoggingEventIdRanges`'s own hard rule ("never renumbering or reassigning an existing domain's base value").
> Implemented and tested (2026-09-03): all four tasks (D-63, C-78, T-62, DO-32) are `●`. `LoggingEventIdRanges.cs` gained `Idempotency = 18000`, `Scheduling = 19000`, `Reporting = 20000`; `LoggingEventIdRangesTests` extended to cover all 21 domain base constants, including a dedicated `PreExistingEighteenDomainConstants_AreByteForByteUnchanged` regression fact hardcoding all 18 prior values (not derived from the same table the theory cases use, so an accidental transposition between two existing constants — which pairwise-uniqueness/modulo checks alone would not catch — fails the test) and a `NewDomainConstants_MatchRootFolderMap` fact for the three new fields; 146/146 `SharedKernel.Primitives.Tests` passing, 72/72 `SharedKernel.Consumer.Tests` unaffected (no regression). `01.Core/README.md`'s registry usage section updated from "00.Governance through 17.Workflows" to "00.Governance through 20.Reporting".
> No work order — direct arch-lead cross-cutting directive.

## Phase: P-487 — Core: Readiness-Probe Primitive for `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P487 -->

> An optional, additive readiness-probe companion contract in `SharedKernel.Cryptography`: `IEncryptionKeyProviderProbe`/`EncryptionKeyProviderHealth`, mirroring the shape `07.Messaging`'s `IMessageBusProbe`/`MessageBusHealth` already established (a plain `Task<THealth>`-returning method, never `ValueTask`, never `Result<T>` — chosen deliberately over `17.Workflows`'s `Task<Result<WorkflowServiceHealth>>` shape and `19.Scheduling`'s zero-I/O bare-`Task<T>` shape, since the phase spec explicitly named `IMessageBusProbe` as the shape to mirror and this probe, like the message-bus one, is genuinely I/O-bound and must never throw for an ordinary reachability failure). Neither `IEncryptionKeyProvider` nor `IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` changes — this is a separate, opt-in contract implemented only by a provider with a real external dependency worth checking. `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` (`SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`) implements it via `ProbeAsync`, which performs exactly one read-only key-metadata lookup (`KeyClient.GetKeyAsync`, the same call `GenerateDataKeyAsync` makes before it ever wraps anything) — never a wrap/unwrap/sign/verify operation, which would register as genuine key usage in Key Vault's own audit trail. Unlike every other member of that class, `ProbeAsync` never lets an Azure SDK exception propagate: it catches every exception except `OperationCanceledException` and reports `EncryptionKeyProviderHealth.IsHealthy = false` with `.Description` set from the exception message — a deliberate, narrow, explicitly-documented carve-out from the class's otherwise-universal fail-closed-via-exception contract, since a readiness probe's purpose is to report status to a health-check pipeline, not to gate a cryptographic operation. The config-based `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider`/`NullEncryptionKeyProvider` (both in `06.Persistence.EfCore`) implement `IEncryptionKeyProvider` but are unaffected by and never required to implement this new probe interface — they have nothing external to check reachability against.
> Closes the blocker recorded against root Phase Backlog P-449 (`13.ServiceDefaults`'s Azure Key Vault composition-root wiring + readiness-probe wiring) — that phase sat `◐` Dispatched, half-shipped, specifically because `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` exposed no `ProbeAsync`-shaped member for `13.ServiceDefaults` to wire into `AddHealthChecks()`. Root `state-map.md`'s P-449 `Depends on` field was updated to `P-447, P-487` by the dispatching pass itself, not by this phase.
> `01.Core` ships this probe primitive only, never an `IHealthCheck` — matching every existing readiness-probe precedent (`06.Persistence`/`08.Storage`/`09.Search`/`10.Intelligence`/`07.Messaging`/`17.Workflows`/`19.Scheduling`). Wiring an implementation into `AddHealthChecks()` remains `13.ServiceDefaults`'s job (P-449).
> Design + implementation combined into this one pass (no separate core-arch-planner design-lock preceded it) — the same "Design + Scaffold + Core + Tests + Docs + Published tracked in one phase section" pattern the `LoggingEventIdRangesNewDomains` phase above used for a similarly small, additive, single-session change.
> WO-080.

## Phase: P-491 — Core: Associated Data (AAD) on `ISymmetricEncryptionService` (BREAKING) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P491 -->

> Every `ISymmetricEncryptionService` member (`Encrypt`/`EncryptAsync`/`Decrypt`/`DecryptAsync`/`EncryptToString(Async)`/`DecryptToString(Async)`) gains a required `byte[] associatedData` parameter, positioned immediately after the primary payload parameter and before `CancellationToken ct = default` on the async members — **no default value, ever**: a caller with no natural context binding must explicitly pass `Array.Empty<byte>()`, never rely on an implicit default. `AesGcmEncryptionService`'s shared `EncryptCore`/`DecryptCore` pair passes `associatedData` straight through to `System.Security.Cryptography.AesGcm.Encrypt`/`.Decrypt`'s own `associatedData` parameter — a BCL AEAD capability already present, simply unused until now. Associated data is authenticated (bound into the AES-GCM tag) but never encrypted and never persisted inside `EncryptedPayload` (no new field) — the caller must be able to reproduce byte-identical AAD at decrypt time from context already available then (a row's own primary key, a cache key, a message type, a subscription id), or authentication fails and `Decrypt`/`DecryptAsync` return `Result.Failure(Error.Unexpected(...))` exactly like any other tamper/wrong-key case — no new `ErrorCodes` constant is needed, this is the same failure shape AES-GCM already produces for a flipped ciphertext/tag byte.
> **Call-site inventory across the six cascading domains (WO-081's own acceptance criterion for this phase)** — the per-domain fix itself ships as each domain's own phase (`02.Caching` P-497, `06.Persistence` P-498, `07.Messaging` P-499, `15.Integration` P-500, `17.Workflows` P-501, `16.Testing` P-502), none of it tracked as a task in this file:
> - `02.Caching/SharedKernel.Caching.FusionCache` — `CacheEncryptionSerializer` (P-433/WO-065). Candidate AAD: the cache key string (UTF-8 bytes) — binds a cached ciphertext to the exact key it was stored under, so a value copied/replayed under a different key fails authentication instead of decrypting cleanly.
> - `06.Persistence/SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` — `EncryptedValueConverter<T>` (P-019, column-level encryption). Candidate AAD: the owning row's primary key. **Flagged as the hardest of the six**: a vanilla EF Core `ValueConverter` has no direct access to the owning entity's PK at conversion time — resolving this (a shadow-property capture, a two-pass materialization, or an accepted narrower AAD such as the table/column name only) is `06.Persistence`'s own P-498 design question, not solved here.
> - `07.Messaging/SharedKernel.Messaging.MassTransit` — the payload-transform `ISerializerFactory`/`IMessageSerializer`/`IMessageDeserializer` decorator trio (P-346/WO-054). Candidate AAD: the message's CLR/CloudEvents type name.
> - `15.Integration/SharedKernel.Integration.Webhooks` — opt-in webhook payload encryption (P-427/WO-064). Candidate AAD: the subscription id.
> - `17.Workflows/SharedKernel.Workflows.Temporal` — `EncryptionPayloadCodec` (AES-256-GCM workflow payload encryption). Candidate AAD: the workflow id (and/or run id).
> - `16.Testing/SharedKernel.Testing` — `AddFakeCryptography()`'s fake `ISymmetricEncryptionService` (P-300/WO-049). Must be migrated to the new signature as a straight compile-time break; the fake may simply thread `associatedData` through to its own round-trip bookkeeping for assertion purposes.
> Design-locked (D-65→D-67, marked `●`); Core/Tests/Docs (C-81, T-66, DO-34) and the breaking repack (P-35) implemented and shipped `●` in a later pass (2026-09-08, core-phase-implementer) — see this file's Changelog for the full summary. All 7/7 tasks now `●`.
> WO-081.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-65 | Design the breaking signature change adding a required `byte[] associatedData` parameter to all eight `ISymmetricEncryptionService` members (`Encrypt`, `EncryptAsync`, `Decrypt`, `DecryptAsync`, `EncryptToString`, `EncryptToStringAsync`, `DecryptToString`, `DecryptToStringAsync`), positioned immediately after the primary payload parameter and before `CancellationToken ct = default` on async members; no default value on any overload — `Array.Empty<byte>()` is an acceptable explicit no-context-binding value, never an implicit one | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-66 | Design `AesGcmEncryptionService`'s shared `EncryptCore`/`DecryptCore` pair to accept `associatedData` and pass it straight through to `AesGcm.Encrypt`/`.Decrypt`'s own `associatedData` parameter; confirm `EncryptedPayload` gains no new field and the packed `EncryptToString`/`DecryptToString` string format never embeds AAD; XML docs on every member state the caller must reproduce byte-identical AAD at decrypt time or authentication fails, returning `Result.Failure(Error.Unexpected(...))` — the same failure shape as any other tamper/wrong-key case, no new `ErrorCodes` constant needed | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-67 | Inventory every production `ISymmetricEncryptionService` call site across the six cascading domains (`02.Caching`, `06.Persistence`, `07.Messaging`, `15.Integration`, `17.Workflows`, `16.Testing`) exactly per the call-site inventory above; record a candidate deterministic AAD derivation for each; flag `06.Persistence`'s `EncryptedValueConverter<T>` as the hardest case and explicitly leave its resolution to `06.Persistence`'s own P-498 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-81 | Implement the breaking signature change across all eight members exactly per D-65/D-66 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| T-66 | Unit: round-trip with matching AAD succeeds; round-trip with mismatched AAD at decrypt fails (`Result.Failure(Error.Unexpected)`), proven by a test that swaps AAD between two otherwise-identical payloads (the phase's headline acceptance criterion); `Array.Empty<byte>()` AAD is a valid, always-succeeding no-context-binding choice; every pre-existing `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests` case updated to pass explicit AAD and re-verified green | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests | `●` |
| DO-34 | XML doc every member's new `associatedData` parameter (authenticated-but-unencrypted, never persisted, caller must reproduce identical bytes at decrypt time); update `01.Core/README.md`'s Symmetric Encryption section with a breaking-change migration table and an AAD usage example (deriving AAD from a cache key/row id/message type/subscription id) | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

## Phase: P-492 — Core: Gate Synchronous `Encrypt`/`Decrypt` Behind a Synchronous-Provider Capability <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P492 -->

> A capability marker, `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider : IEncryptionKeyProvider` (zero-member marker interface), implemented only by a provider that can genuinely guarantee `GetCurrentKeyAsync`/`GetKeyAsync` never perform a blocking network/IPC round trip — the config/environment-backed default. A new static class, `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities`, exposes `IsGenuinelySynchronous(IEncryptionKeyProvider provider) → bool`. The four retained synchronous `ISymmetricEncryptionService` members (`Encrypt`/`Decrypt`/`EncryptToString`/`DecryptToString`) compute this check **once**, at `AesGcmEncryptionService` construction time (cached as a private `bool` field — the registered provider instance is immutable for the service's lifetime), and throw a clear, structural `NotSupportedException` directing the caller to the `*Async` overloads when the check is false, instead of silently bridging via `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()`. Behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged when the registered provider genuinely is synchronous (zero behavior change for the common config-backed case).
> **`CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`'s relationship to the marker, decided explicitly (the phase's own named acceptance criterion):** `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` itself **never** directly implements `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` — its own true synchronous-safety is entirely a function of what it wraps (a cache *hit* is always fast regardless of the inner provider, but a cache *miss* re-enters the inner provider, and if that inner provider genuinely blocks on network I/O, so does the decorator on that path). `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` gains a new public read-only `Inner → IEncryptionKeyProvider` property exposing the wrapped instance specifically so `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous` can see through the decorator: it returns `true` when the provider implements `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` directly, **or** when the provider is a `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` whose `.Inner` also (recursively) satisfies this same check — `false` for every other case, including any unrecognized third-party decorator type (the check fails toward requiring the `*Async` overloads, never toward silently blocking). This is the resolution to "the check must reflect the INNER provider's true nature, never the decorator's best-case behavior": naively treating `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` as always-fast because it caches would be exactly that forbidden decorator's-best-case assumption.
> A hand-rolled KMS-backed `IEncryptionKeyProvider` implementer must never implement `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` unless its author can genuinely assert synchronous safety — this marker is opt-in, never inferred.
> This same gating pattern (a capability marker + a static `IsGenuinelySynchronous` helper) is reused again by P-493 for `IAsymmetricSignatureService`'s own retained sync `Sign`/`Verify`, with its own analogous marker (`ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider`) — "reused" means the architectural pattern, not literally this same interface, since `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` is an unrelated contract from `IEncryptionKeyProvider`.
> Design-locked in this pass (D-68→D-70, marked `●`); Core/Tests/Docs/Repack (C-82/C-83, T-67/T-68, DO-35, P-36) implemented and shipped — see Changelog.
> Depends on P-491 landing in the same or an earlier repack (both touch `AesGcmEncryptionService`/`ISymmetricEncryptionService`; sequencing avoids a second churn of the same file, though the two phases' contracts are otherwise independent).
> WO-081.

|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-68 | Define `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider : IEncryptionKeyProvider` (zero-member marker interface); XML docs state IN CAPITALS that a KMS/HSM-backed provider must never implement this marker, and that implementing it is an explicit, author-asserted safety claim, never inferred | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-69 | Define `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous(IEncryptionKeyProvider provider) → bool` exactly per the recursive-unwrap design above; add `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider.Inner → IEncryptionKeyProvider` (public read-only, exposes the wrapped instance); confirm `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` itself never directly implements `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-70 | Design the four retained sync `ISymmetricEncryptionService` members to compute `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous` once at `AesGcmEncryptionService` construction (cached `bool` field) and throw `NotSupportedException` directing to the `*Async` overloads when false, replacing the unconditional `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()` bridge; zero behavior change when the registered provider is genuinely synchronous | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

## Phase: P-493 — Core: `IAsymmetricKeyProvider`/`IAsymmetricSignatureService` Go Async; Fix Key Ownership and Verify Parity (BREAKING) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P493 -->

> `IAsymmetricKeyProvider.GetRsaKey`/`GetEcdsaKey` become `GetRsaKeyAsync(string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<RSA>` / `GetEcdsaKeyAsync(string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<ECDsa>` — the prior synchronous members are **removed outright**, not kept as a parallel overload, mirroring P-446's `IEncryptionKeyProvider` precedent exactly; `KeyNotFoundException` for an unknown `keyId` propagates through the `ValueTask` exactly as it propagated synchronously before (no adoption of `Result<T>` for this contract — unchanged failure shape).
> A second capability marker, `ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider : IAsymmetricKeyProvider` (zero-member, the asymmetric-signing analog of P-492's `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider`), and a matching static helper, `AsymmetricKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous(IAsymmetricKeyProvider provider) → bool` — a **direct marker check only** in this phase, since no caching decorator exists yet for `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` (a future one, if ever added, must extend this helper the same recursive-unwrap way P-492 extended `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities` for `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`). `IAsymmetricSignatureService` gains `SignAsync(byte[] data, string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<byte[]>` / `VerifyAsync(byte[] data, byte[] signature, string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<bool>`; the retained sync `Sign`/`Verify` are gated by `AsymmetricKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous` exactly per P-492's pattern, throwing `NotSupportedException` when ungated, documented IN CAPITALS as blocking a real thread otherwise.
> **A genuine BCL limitation this phase cannot fix, recorded explicitly rather than glossed over:** `System.Security.Cryptography.RSA`/`ECDsa`'s `SignData`/`VerifyData` have **no async overload anywhere in the BCL** — so even inside `SignAsync`/`VerifyAsync`, once the (now-async) key resolution completes, the actual cryptographic sign/verify call against the returned `RSA`/`ECDsa` instance is inherently synchronous. For a remote-KMS-backed key (P-494), that means the sign/verify call itself still performs a real, unavoidable blocking network round trip internally — this phase makes **key resolution** async, never the signing primitive itself. A future maintainer must not assume `SignAsync` is fully non-blocking end to end for every provider; P-494's design carries this same warning forward at the point where it becomes concretely observable.
> `RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService` stop disposing the RSA/ECDsa instance the provider hands back — the `using` around it is removed; XML docs on `IAsymmetricKeyProvider.GetRsaKeyAsync`/`GetEcdsaKeyAsync` state explicitly the returned instance is **not caller-owned**. This fixes a latent defect (a `using`-scoped `RSA`/`ECDsa` disposes a key instance the provider may still hold and reuse — an immediate `ObjectDisposedException` on the very next call under any sensible caching/pooling provider implementation, including P-494's) that was invisible only because no provider implementation existed yet to make it reachable. `Verify` also gains the same `EnsureMinimumKeySize` check `Sign` already applies, for both `RsaSignatureService` and `EcdsaSignatureService` — closing a silent accept-anything gap on the more security-sensitive operation (verifying an attacker-supplied signature against an undersized key was previously never rejected for key-size reasons).
> **Shipped end to end (12/12).** Design-locked (D-71→D-74), then Core/Tests/Docs (C-84→C-87, T-69/T-70, DO-36) and the breaking repack (P-37) all implemented and green: 294/294 `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests` passing (up from 267), repacked at `1.0.0-alpha.0.854` (MinVer — no `<Version>` added; a real MAJOR git-tag bump is `devops-lead`'s call, per the P-491/P-492 precedent).
> Depended on P-492 (reused its gating pattern and, indirectly, its `AesGcmEncryptionService`/`ISymmetricEncryptionService` repack sequencing).
> WO-081.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-71 | Design the breaking `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` signature change — `GetRsaKeyAsync(string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<RSA>`, `GetEcdsaKeyAsync(string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<ECDsa>`; the two synchronous members are removed, not retained as an overload; `KeyNotFoundException` for an unknown `keyId` propagates through the `ValueTask` exactly as before | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-72 | Define `ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider : IAsymmetricKeyProvider` (zero-member marker, mirroring D-68's `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider`) and `AsymmetricKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous(IAsymmetricKeyProvider provider) → bool` (direct marker check only — no decorator-unwrapping logic yet, since no caching decorator exists for `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` in this phase) | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-73 | Design `IAsymmetricSignatureService.SignAsync`/`VerifyAsync` exactly per the shapes above; the retained sync `Sign`/`Verify` gated by `AsymmetricKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous` exactly per P-492's pattern, throwing `NotSupportedException` when ungated, IN CAPITALS blocking-warning otherwise; explicitly record the BCL RSA/ECDsa-has-no-async-sign-primitive limitation in XML docs so a future maintainer does not assume `SignAsync` is fully non-blocking for every provider | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| D-74 | Design the `RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService` key-ownership fix — remove the `using` around the provider-returned RSA/ECDsa instance, document it as not caller-owned; design `Verify`'s `EnsureMinimumKeySize` parity fix (both RSA and ECDSA) matching `Sign`'s existing check | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

## Phase: P-494 — Core: Azure Key Vault Keys Remote-Signing `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` Implementation <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P494 -->

> A new class, `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` (sealed, implements `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` only) inside the existing `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` package — deliberately **a separate class from `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`**, since signing keys (used directly for sign/verify) and wrap/unwrap keys are a different Key Vault key usage pattern even when both live in the same vault; reuses the existing `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions.VaultUri`/`.KeyNames`/`.Credential` — no new options type. `GetRsaKeyAsync`/`GetEcdsaKeyAsync` back Azure Key Vault Keys' remote sign/verify operations (`CryptographyClient.SignData`/`VerifyData`) rather than exporting private key material Key Vault does not release. The returned RSA/ECDsa instance is a thin internal-sealed subclass (`KeyVaultRsaKey : RSA`, `KeyVaultEcdsaKey : ECDsa`) whose `SignData`/`VerifyData` overrides delegate to the remote call, so `RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService` (P-493) consume it through the exact same contract as a local key, with **zero changes to those two classes beyond what P-493 already introduced**. `ExportParameters`/`ImportParameters` throw `NotSupportedException` on both subclasses — private key material never crosses the process boundary. `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` never implements `ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider` — it always performs genuine network I/O.
> **Bakes in P-496's connection-reuse fix from day one**: `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` caches one `CryptographyClient` per distinct Azure key name (never constructed per call) from its very first implementation, rather than repeating the per-call-construction defect P-496 has to retroactively correct in the older `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`.
> **The (HashAlgorithmName, RSASignaturePadding) → Azure `SignatureAlgorithm` mapping is narrow by design**: `KeyVaultRsaKey.SignData`/`VerifyData` map SHA-256+PSS to Azure's `PS256` (matching `RsaSignatureService`'s existing fixed choice) and throw `NotSupportedException` for any other combination; `KeyVaultEcdsaKey` maps SHA-256 on the P-256 curve to `ES256` (matching `EcdsaSignatureService`'s existing fixed choice) the same way — this class is not a general-purpose algorithm-negotiation surface, it exists to serve exactly the two fixed algorithm choices `RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService` already make.
> **Carries forward P-493's BCL-limitation warning at the point it becomes concretely observable**: `KeyVaultRsaKey.SignData`/`.VerifyData` (and their ECDsa equivalents) perform a real, unavoidable blocking network call to Key Vault — Azure's SDK offers genuine synchronous `CryptographyClient.SignData`/`VerifyData` methods (not a `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()` bridge over the async ones, since `RSA`/`ECDsa`'s BCL surface gives this class no async entry point to bridge from in the first place), but the call is still synchronous I/O either way. XML docs on both subclasses state this IN CAPITALS.
> This completes the existing Azure provider family's capability set (already implements `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider`/`IEncryptionKeyProviderProbe`) rather than adding a new cloud provider; no AWS KMS or HashiCorp Vault provider is introduced by this phase.
> Design-locked in this pass (D-75/D-76, marked `●`); Core/Tests/Docs (C-88/C-89, T-71, DO-37) and the additive repack (P-38) remain `○` pending a future implementation pass.
> Depends on P-493 (`IAsymmetricKeyProvider` must already be async before this provider can implement it).
> WO-081.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-75 | Define `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` (sealed, implements `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` only, distinct from `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`, reuses `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions`); design its per-key-name `CryptographyClient` cache (a `ConcurrentDictionary<string, CryptographyClient>`, created at most once per distinct Azure key name, never per call) — the same pattern D-80/P-496 later applies retroactively to `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`; never implements `ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider` | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| D-76 | Define `KeyVaultRsaKey : RSA` / `KeyVaultEcdsaKey : ECDsa` (internal sealed) — **CORRECTED post-implementation (C-88/C-89, WO-081): the overrides are `SignHash`/`VerifyHash`, NOT `SignData`/`VerifyData`.** `RSA.SignData`/`VerifyData` and `ECDsa.SignData`/`VerifyData` are non-virtual convenience methods that hash the input locally and then call `SignHash`/`VerifyHash` internally — attempting `override` on `SignData`/`VerifyData` directly fails to compile (CS0506), exactly the same BCL characteristic P-493's own test-double implementation already discovered (see `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests/Signing/DisposeGuardedKeys.cs`). The real overrides delegate to `CryptographyClient`'s real synchronous `Sign`/`Verify` methods (the hash-taking overloads — `CryptographyClient.Sign(SignatureAlgorithm, byte[] digest, ...)`/`.Verify(SignatureAlgorithm, byte[] digest, byte[] signature, ...)` — confirmed via direct reflection against the installed 4.7.0 assembly, not assumed), mapping the fixed (HashAlgorithmName, RSASignaturePadding)/(HashAlgorithmName) combination each signature service actually requests to the matching Azure `SignatureAlgorithm` (`PS256` for RSA+SHA-256+PSS, `ES256` for ECDSA+SHA-256 on P-256 — for `ECDsa.SignHash(byte[] hash)`, which carries no algorithm parameter at all, the 32-byte SHA-256 digest length is the discriminant instead), throwing `NotSupportedException` for any other combination; `ExportParameters`/`ImportParameters` throw `NotSupportedException` on both; XML docs state IN CAPITALS that both overrides perform a real, unavoidable blocking network call. This row's original text (the design-lock pass's assumed `SignData`/`VerifyData` shape) was left uncorrected until implementation — treat this corrected text as authoritative | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| C-88 | Implement `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` exactly per D-75 | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| C-89 | Implement `KeyVaultRsaKey`/`KeyVaultEcdsaKey` exactly per D-76 (corrected `SignHash`/`VerifyHash` shape); extend `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` to additionally register `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` as `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` (a distinct singleton from `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`) | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| T-71 | Integration (mirrors T-54's/T-65's existing skip-if-unavailable-gated real-vault posture): `GetRsaKeyAsync`/`GetEcdsaKeyAsync` round-trip via `RsaSignatureService.SignAsync`/`VerifyAsync` and `EcdsaSignatureService.SignAsync`/`VerifyAsync` against a real (env-gated via `SHAREDKERNEL_TEST_AZURE_KEYVAULT_URI`/`_RSA_SIGNING_KEY_NAME`/`_ECDSA_SIGNING_KEY_NAME`, skips honestly when unset — not exercised in this sandbox) Key Vault key, without ever exporting private key material; `ExportParameters`/`ImportParameters` throw `NotSupportedException`; genuinely-always-executed unreachable-vault fail-closed coverage (`AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProviderIntegrationTests`) plus locally-decidable unit coverage (`AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProviderTests`: unknown-keyId `KeyNotFoundException`, never-`ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider`, sync `Sign`/`Verify` throwing `NotSupportedException`) — 53/53 tests green, 0 requiring a real vault | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure.Tests | `●` |

| P-38 | Pack and publish `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` with a MINOR version bump (additive — a new provider class, a new DI registration), sequenced after P-493's breaking `SharedKernel.Cryptography` repack; re-verify the `.nuspec` dependency confinement (`Azure.Security.KeyVault.Keys`/`Azure.Identity` never leak onto `SharedKernel.Cryptography` itself — re-confirmed by direct nuspec inspection of both packages). Packed to the local feed (`SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure.1.0.0-alpha.0.858.nupkg`); the real release tag remains `devops-lead`'s call, per WO-081's established P-491/P-492/P-493 precedent. **Honest note on local-feed state:** `01.Core/SharedKernel.Consumer.Tests` was found ALREADY failing to restore against this sandbox's local `nupkgs/` feed before this phase touched anything (`SharedKernel.DataPrivacy`/`SharedKernel.Localization` nupkgs are simply absent from this ephemeral, gitignored directory, and the `SharedKernel.Cryptography` chain's own minimum-version floors already exceeded what was packed for `SharedKernel.Primitives`/`SharedKernel.Configuration`) — a pre-existing, out-of-phase-scope gap, not something P-494 introduced or is positioned to fix | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |

## Phase: P-495 — New Package: `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2` <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P495 -->

> A new, **thirteenth `01.Core` package**, `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2`, mirroring the `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`/`SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation` sibling-with-third-party-dependency precedent exactly: `Argon2idOneWayHasher` (sealed, implements `IOneWayHasher`) using a maintained Argon2id NuGet package, registered as a **keyed alternative** ("Argon2id") alongside the unkeyed PBKDF2 default — never replacing it. `SharedKernel.Cryptography` core stays zero-third-party-dependency; `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`/`CryptographyOptions.Pbkdf2Iterations` are completely untouched by this phase.
> **Self-describing output format**: the real, interoperable **PHC string format** (`$argon2id$v=19$m=<memoryKb>,t=<iterations>,p=<parallelism>$<base64 salt>$<base64 subkey>`) — the industry-standard Argon2 hash-storage shape, not a bespoke encoding, chosen deliberately over inventing a new format the way `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher` did (PBKDF2 has no equivalently universal standard string format; Argon2 does, and departing from it would forfeit interoperability for no benefit). `Verify` parses the PHC string, recomputes under the same parsed parameters, constant-time-compares the subkey (`CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals`, never `==`/`SequenceEqual`), and returns `HashVerificationResult.SuccessRehashNeeded` when the parsed `m`/`t`/`p` differ from the currently-configured `Argon2CryptographyOptions` — the exact same rehash-on-parameter-change shape `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher` already uses.
> **`Argon2CryptographyOptions`** (bound via `IOptions<T>`, validated via `AddValidatedOptions`): `.MemorySizeKb` (default `19456` = 19 MiB), `.Iterations` (default `2`), `.DegreeOfParallelism` (default `1`) — OWASP Password Storage Cheat Sheet's current minimum recommended Argon2id configuration, cited explicitly (mirrors `Pbkdf2Iterations`'s "600,000 (OWASP 2023+ guidance)" precedent). `AddSharedKernelArgon2Cryptography(IConfiguration configuration)` registers the validated options and `Argon2idOneWayHasher` as a **keyed-only singleton** (`Argon2CryptographyServiceCollectionExtensions.Argon2idOneWayHasherKey` = `"Argon2id"`) — never as the unkeyed `IOneWayHasher` default, mirroring `GZipPayloadCompressor`'s keyed-only-no-unkeyed-registration precedent exactly.
> **Third-party dependency**: `Konscious.Security.Cryptography.Argon2` — a maintained, pure-managed .NET implementation, chosen over a native/libsodium-backed binding to avoid compounding this platform's AOT-preferred posture with a P/Invoke dependency on top of an already-third-party choice; AOT status flagged, not blocked on, exactly like `FluentValidation`'s and the Azure SDK's existing precedent. Confined entirely to this new package, verified via the established `.nuspec`-inspection technique in `SharedKernel.Consumer.Tests`.
> Package README documents when to choose Argon2id vs. PBKDF2 — **FIPS-mode is the deciding factor**: PBKDF2 stays the FIPS-approved-algorithm-territory choice (and remains the unkeyed default for exactly this reason); Argon2id is the OWASP-preferred choice everywhere FIPS is not a hard constraint.
> Design-locked in this pass (D-77→D-79, marked `●`); Scaffold/Core/Tests/Docs/Published (S-25, C-90/C-91, T-72, DO-38, P-39→P-41) remain `○` pending a future implementation pass. Full package lifecycle tracked in one phase section, mirroring the `SharedKernel.Compression`/`SharedKernel.DataPrivacy` new-package precedent.
> No dependency on any other WO-081 phase — this phase can implement independently of P-491→P-494/P-496.
> WO-081.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-77 | Define `Argon2idOneWayHasher` (sealed, implements `IOneWayHasher`) — `Hash(string secret)` generates a cryptographically random salt via `ISecureRandomGenerator` and emits the PHC string format above; `Verify(string hash, string secret)` parses it, recomputes under the parsed parameters, constant-time-compares the subkey, and returns `SuccessRehashNeeded` when parsed `m`/`t`/`p` differ from the current `Argon2CryptographyOptions`; a malformed/foreign-algorithm string returns `HashVerificationResult.Failed`, never throws | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2 | `●` |
| D-78 | Define `Argon2CryptographyOptions` (`.MemorySizeKb` default `19456`, `.Iterations` default `2`, `.DegreeOfParallelism` default `1`, citing OWASP's current Argon2id minimum recommendation) and `AddSharedKernelArgon2Cryptography(IConfiguration configuration)` — registers the validated options and `Argon2idOneWayHasher` as the `"Argon2id"`-keyed singleton only, never the unkeyed `IOneWayHasher` default; confirm `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`/`CryptographyOptions.Pbkdf2Iterations` remain completely unchanged | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2 | `●` |

## Phase: P-496 — Core: Azure Key Vault Provider Hardening — Connection Reuse, Compact Key Identifiers, a Real Rotation Story <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P496 -->

> Three related redesigns to the already-shipped `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`:
> **(1) Connection reuse** — a single, shared `CryptographyClient` per master key (a `ConcurrentDictionary<string, CryptographyClient>` keyed by Azure key name, created at most once, reused for every subsequent wrap/unwrap/GetKey call), never constructed per call — restores HTTP connection pooling. Mirrors `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider`'s own cache, which P-494 already baked in from its first implementation specifically to avoid repeating this defect twice.
> **(2) Compact, durable, rotating key identifiers, with a corrected design assumption from the original P-447 shipment** — `CryptographicKey.Id` becomes a short opaque version tag (`"v1"`, `"v2"`, …) instead of the previous self-decodable ~470-byte envelope. This surfaces and fixes a real, previously-undocumented gap: today's "process-lifetime cached current data key" behavior means **every process/pod/replica silently mints its own unique local AES-256 data key** on first use, with no genuine rotation intent behind it and no sharing across replicas of the same service — "current key" was never actually a shared, stable concept, just a per-process accident. P-496 replaces this with a **durable, Key-Vault-Secrets-backed version registry**: a new `Azure.Security.KeyVault.Secrets` client (confined to this package — a second Azure SDK family alongside the existing `Azure.Security.KeyVault.Keys`) stores each version's wrapped-DEK bytes + originating Azure master-key id as a Key Vault secret named by its short tag, plus a single `"current version"` pointer secret every replica reads, so "current" becomes a genuinely shared, deliberately-minted concept rather than a per-process accident. A `ConcurrentDictionary<string, byte[]>` memoizes a version tag's already-unwrapped plaintext data key for the remainder of the process's lifetime once resolved once — a second decrypt citing an already-seen tag costs zero further Key Vault calls (`GetKeyAsync`'s own stated acceptance criterion).
> **Backward-read compatibility, a deliberate design choice avoiding a forced data migration:** `GetKeyAsync` first attempts the new short-tag registry lookup; if the supplied `keyId` does not match the new tag format (e.g. does not match a `"v{N}"`-shaped pattern), it falls back to the **legacy self-decodable envelope parsing path** the original P-447 shipment used, so any already-persisted row encrypted under the pre-P-496 shape remains decryptable indefinitely with no forced migration. `GetCurrentKeyAsync` (used only for new encryption going forward) always mints/returns the new short-tag shape. Because reads stay backward-compatible and no public member's signature changes (`CryptographicKey.Id` is still just `string`), **this phase ships as an additive/MINOR repack, not a breaking one** — a deliberate design choice, not an oversight; the alternative (a breaking repack mandating a data migration for every service with existing encrypted rows) was considered and rejected.
> **(3) A real, explicitly-callable rotation story** — a new public method, `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider.MintNewVersionAsync(CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<string>` (returns the new version tag; **not** part of `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`IEnvelopeEncryptionProvider` — a provider-specific operational surface, mirroring `06.Persistence`'s `IEncryptionRotationJob` precedent of leaving scheduling to the caller). Generates a fresh local AES-256 data key, wraps it via the master key, stores it as a new version secret, and atomically repoints the `"current version"` secret to the new tag; every previously-minted version's secret is left untouched and remains resolvable via `GetKeyAsync(oldTag)` indefinitely. Automatic/policy-driven rotation scheduling is deliberately out of scope — callable only, from an ops script, a hosted job, or a future `19.Scheduling` job, mirroring how `SharedKernel.DataPrivacy`'s cross-service erasure orchestrator was left out of P-474.
> **`EnvelopeDataKey.MasterKeyId` simplification (envelope-wrap path, distinct from the direct-retrieval path above)** — since `UnwrapDataKeyAsync`'s caller already supplies the actual `WrappedKey` bytes directly (never round-tripped through this provider's own durable store), `MasterKeyId` never needed the oversized self-decodable envelope shape at all; it becomes the real (short) Azure key name + version identifier. This requires no new durable registry — unlike the direct-retrieval path, the envelope path's *caller* is already the durable store for the wrapped bytes.
> **`CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`'s working set is now genuinely bounded** — documented explicitly: every replica resolving `"current"` down to the same shared, deliberately-minted tag (rather than one unique tag per process restart) is what actually bounds a wrapping `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`'s dictionary by live-key-version count (a small, deliberate, ops-driven number) instead of by pod-restart count (unbounded over a service's whole operational history).
> Design-locked in this pass (D-80→D-83, marked `●`); Core/Tests/Docs (C-92→C-95, T-73/T-74, DO-39) and the additive repack (P-42) remain `○` pending a future implementation pass.
> Depends on P-494 (both touch `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s internals; sequencing after P-494 lets this phase reuse the exact `CryptographyClient`-per-key-name caching pattern P-494 already introduced for the sibling signing provider, rather than inventing it twice independently).
> WO-081.
