## Phase: P-510 — Core: `ResultTry` Exception-Message Redaction and Cancellation Passthrough (SEVERE) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P510 -->

> `ResultTry.Try`/`TryAsync`'s default (no custom `onException`) exception mapping stops interpolating a caught exception's raw `"{ExceptionType}: {ExceptionMessage}"` into `Error.Message` — that text can carry a raw driver/SDK error string (e.g. a Postgres error embedding a connection-string fragment or username) which then travels through ordinary `Result<T>` plumbing straight to `Error.ToProblemDetails()` with nothing downstream aware it originated from a caught exception, bypassing the redaction `SharedKernelExceptionHandler` already applies to a THROWN exception outside Development. The default mapping now always produces one fixed, safe `Error.Unexpected(ErrorCodes.Unexpected.Default, SafeMessage)` string.
> **Where the raw exception text goes instead — honoring, not working around, this same audit's ratified `⊘ DECLINED` "no metadata bag on `Error`" ruling (root `state-map.md`, 2026-09-04):** `Error` stays `(Code, Message, Type)` exactly as ruled — no new field, no bag. Before constructing the safe `Error`, `MapException` calls `System.Diagnostics.Activity.Current?.AddException(exception)` — a real .NET 8+ BCL member on `Activity` (part of the shared framework already, zero new NuGet dependency) that records the exception as a structured OTel-semantic-convention event on the ambient trace span. This is the "channel that never reaches `Error`" the dispatched brief asked for: exception detail flows through the SAME ambient trace-context channel this platform's own Logging Conventions already use for CorrelationId/TraceId/SpanId (root `CLAUDE.md`: "flow ambiently through the OpenTelemetry logging pipeline"), consumed by `13.ServiceDefaults`'s already-wired trace export — never serialized into the HTTP response by this package. When `Activity.Current` is `null` (no active span), the exception detail is recorded nowhere — a documented, accepted limitation; a caller wanting a guaranteed capture path supplies its own `onException` mapper, which is entirely unaffected by this change and receives the raw exception exactly as before.
> `AggregateException` flattening (existing behavior) is preserved exactly — every inner exception is still individually represented — only the DESTINATION changes: one `Activity.AddException` call per flattened inner exception, instead of joining their type+message strings into `Error.Message`.
> Separately: every `catch (Exception exception)` across all FOUR members (`Try`, `Try` w/ custom mapper, `TryAsync`, `TryAsync` w/ custom mapper) narrows to `catch (Exception exception) when (exception is not OperationCanceledException)` — applies uniformly, including the custom-mapper overloads, since a genuine cancellation must never be handed to ANY mapper for conversion into a `Result` — it must always propagate as a thrown exception, exactly like every other `async`/`await` call site on this platform.
> Two real, narrow BREAKING BEHAVIOR CHANGES (not a compile-time API break — no signature changes): (a) a caller relying on the old default `Error.Message` containing the raw exception text now sees a fixed safe string instead; (b) a caller relying on the old bare `catch (Exception)` swallowing `OperationCanceledException` into a `Result.Failure` now observes a thrown exception. Release notes must state both explicitly — mirrors P-492's precedent for a narrow, non-signature-breaking behavior change.
> Depends on: None. No dependency on any other WO-083 phase or on `Error`/`ErrorCodes` changing.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-84 | Design the safe fixed default `Error.Message`, the `Activity.Current?.AddException(...)` telemetry channel (including per-inner-exception recording for a flattened `AggregateException`), and the `catch (Exception) when (exception is not OperationCanceledException)` narrowing across all four `ResultTry` members, exactly per the design above; explicitly record that this satisfies the ratified "no metadata bag on `Error`" constraint by using `Activity` as the channel instead of extending `Error` | SharedKernel.Core | `●` |
| C-96 | Implement exactly per D-84 | SharedKernel.Core | `●` |
| T-75 | Unit tests: the default mapping's `Error.Message` is asserted to equal the fixed safe string (never merely "does not contain" the raw text) for both a single exception and a flattened `AggregateException`; a custom `onException` mapper's `Error.Message` is unaffected byte-for-byte; a real `ActivityListener` proves `Activity.Current.AddException` is genuinely invoked once per flattened inner exception (not a mock); `OperationCanceledException` (and `TaskCanceledException`, its BCL subclass) thrown inside the delegate propagates uncaught from all four overloads, including the custom-mapper ones | SharedKernel.Core.Tests | `●` |
| DO-40 | XML doc every changed member; state IN CAPITALS that the raw exception text is available only via the ambient `Activity`/OTel channel, never via the returned `Error`; update `01.Core/README.md`'s `ResultTry` section with a breaking-behavior-change note (message content + cancellation propagation) and a short "reading exception detail from traces" example | SharedKernel.Core | `●` |
| P-43 | Repack `SharedKernel.Core`; release notes state both narrow breaking behavior changes explicitly (default message content, `OperationCanceledException` no longer swallowed) | SharedKernel.Core | `●` |

## Phase: P-511 — Core: Fix Cancellation-Token Leak in Single-Flight Key-Resolution Caches <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P511 -->

> **Verified against real source, not assumed — the dispatched brief's named target has already moved:** the brief names `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s "current key" process-lifetime slot as the second fix site. Reading `01.Core/SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure/AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider.cs` as it stands after P-496 (SHIPPED, same WO-081 batch) confirms that exact slot no longer exists — P-496 replaced it with a durable Key-Vault-Secrets-backed version registry plus TWO NEW `Lazy<Task<T>>`-shaped caches: `_resolvedKeysByCacheKey`/`ResolveAsync` (a `ConcurrentDictionary<string, Lazy<Task<ResolvedAzureKey>>>`) and `_plaintextKeysByTag`/`GetOrAddMemoizedPlaintextKeyAsync` (a `ConcurrentDictionary<string, Lazy<Task<byte[]>>>`). Both reproduce the IDENTICAL captured-caller-token defect shape the original finding described: each `Lazy<Task<T>>` factory closure captures the CancellationToken of whichever caller's `GetOrAdd` call happened to win the race to construct the slot, and every other concurrent caller then awaits that exact same shared `Task` via `.Value` — so that one winning caller's cancellation can fault or cancel every other caller's still-legitimate, still-in-flight request.
> **`CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`'s own `GetOrRefreshAsync`/`CacheSlot`** (in `SharedKernel.Cryptography`, untouched by P-496) is confirmed independently to have the exact same shape — this is the site the brief named correctly and it is fixed as originally scoped.
> **A THIRD, independently-discovered site, not named in the brief at all — SCOPE CORRECTED to include it:** `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` (P-494, SHIPPED, same `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` package family) has the IDENTICAL shape in its own `ResolveAsync`/`_resolvedKeysByAzureKeyName`. Fixing the two named sites while knowingly leaving an identical defect unfixed in a sibling class of the exact same package, discovered while auditing the named sites, would be an inconsistent audit — this phase's scope is corrected to a fourth fix site total: `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` (as named) + `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s two caches (the brief's named site, relocated) + `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` (newly discovered).
> **The fix, applied uniformly to all four cache sites:** (1) a dedicated `CancellationTokenSource` owned by the cache slot/entry itself — NEVER derived from, linked to, or constructed from any individual caller's token — drives the shared inner factory call; (2) each caller awaits the shared `Task` via the BCL's own `Task.WaitAsync(callerCt)` (.NET 6+), which surfaces `OperationCanceledException` to THAT CALLER ONLY when its own token fires, without disturbing the underlying shared `Task` or any other awaiter observing the same `Task`; (3) a per-slot reference count of currently-awaiting callers, incremented before each `WaitAsync` and decremented in a `finally` once that caller's wait completes (success, fault, or that caller's own cancellation) — the slot's dedicated `CancellationTokenSource` is cancelled only once this count reaches zero, so the inner provider call is genuinely aborted once abandoned by every caller, never while at least one caller is still legitimately waiting. Cancelling the dedicated source after the shared `Task` has already completed is a harmless no-op — it can never disturb an already-produced result, so ordering races between "last caller leaves" and "operation completes" are safe by construction.
> This satisfies all four dispatched acceptance criteria: caller A's own cancellation never cancels or faults caller B's concurrent await of the same shared resolution (each caller observes only its own token via `WaitAsync`); the underlying inner-provider call remains genuinely cancellable once truly abandoned (refcount-to-zero triggers the dedicated source); every other P-446 single-flight/fail-closed/no-stale-fallback guarantee is untouched — only WHICH token governs cancellation of the shared work changes, not the shared work's own success/failure/no-fallback semantics; a concurrency test with staggered caller cancellation is buildable against all four sites using the identical fixture shape.
> Depends on: None as a WO-083 phase-ordering matter. Touches code P-494/P-496 already shipped (both `●`) without reopening either phase's own already-closed design decisions.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-85 | Design the dedicated-`CancellationTokenSource` + `Task.WaitAsync(callerCt)` + refcounted-abandonment-cancellation pattern exactly per the design above, applied uniformly to all four confirmed sites: (1) `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider.GetOrRefreshAsync`'s `CacheSlot` (`SharedKernel.Cryptography`), (2) `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s `_resolvedKeysByCacheKey`/`ResolveAsync`, (3) `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s `_plaintextKeysByTag`/`GetOrAddMemoizedPlaintextKeyAsync` (both `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`), and (4) `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider`'s `_resolvedKeysByAzureKeyName`/`ResolveAsync` (`SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`) — explicitly record the scope correction from the dispatched brief in this task's own notes | SharedKernel.Cryptography, SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |
| C-97 | Implement the fix in `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` exactly per D-85 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-98 | Implement the fix in `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` exactly per D-85, both cache sites (`_resolvedKeysByCacheKey`, `_plaintextKeysByTag`) | SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure | `●` |

## Phase: P-512 — Core: PBKDF2 Iteration Floor and Verify-Time Ceiling <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P512 -->

> **Floor:** `CryptographyOptions.Pbkdf2Iterations` is validated today only by `[Range(1, int.MaxValue)]` — a configured value of `1` passes `AddValidatedOptions`/`ValidateOnStart()` cleanly, defeating the point of a slow KDF entirely. Add `public const int MinimumPbkdf2Iterations = 100_000;` and change the attribute to `[Range(MinimumPbkdf2Iterations, int.MaxValue)]`. 100,000 is chosen well below the package's own 600,000 OWASP-2023+ default (so the floor never becomes the practical ceiling for a correctly-configured service) and well above `1` — enforced entirely through the ALREADY-WIRED `AddValidatedOptions`/`ValidateOnStart()` path (`CryptographyServiceCollectionExtensions.AddSharedKernelCryptography`); a plain DataAnnotations `[Range]` is sufficient, no new `IValidateOptions<T>` implementation needed.
> **Verify-time ceiling — the real compatibility constraint this phase must respect:** `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher.Verify` reads `storedIterations` out of the hash blob itself precisely so raising the configured count never invalidates already-stored hashes (the package's own documented self-describing-format design). A ceiling that is too tight would reject legitimately-stored older hashes, breaking that guarantee. The ceiling therefore must be a FIXED CONSTANT set independently of `CryptographyOptions.Pbkdf2Iterations`'s currently-configured value — never "whatever is configured today plus a margin," since a future legitimate increase to the configured default must never require a simultaneous ceiling bump, and the ceiling must not be reachable or movable via ordinary configuration at all (a stored hash's iteration count is attacker-influenceable — anyone who can write a hash row can write an absurd one). `private const int MaxVerifiableIterations = 2_000_000;` in `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher` — generous (>3x the shipped 600,000 default) enough that no hash ever legitimately produced by `Hash()` at any historically-plausible configured iteration count is ever rejected, while still bounding the CPU an attacker-supplied blob can force `Verify` to spend. The check happens BEFORE `Rfc2898DeriveBytes.Pbkdf2` is ever called — checking after would defeat the purpose, since the expensive call would already have run.
> **Deliberately NOT enforcing the new 100,000 floor on stored/verified values** — only at `Hash()` time, on newly-configured values. A hash legitimately created under an older, lower-than-100,000 configuration (this package shipped with NO floor at all until this phase) must continue to verify — this generalizes the dispatched acceptance criterion "existing valid hashes at the current 600,000-iteration default continue to verify correctly" to any pre-floor legitimate value below the new ceiling.
> **Subkey-length ceiling (a second, independent CPU/memory-exhaustion vector, found while designing the first):** `TryDecode` today treats "everything after the salt" as the subkey with no length check at all — `Rfc2898DeriveBytes.Pbkdf2`'s last parameter (`expectedSubkey.Length`) becomes the REQUESTED DERIVED-KEY LENGTH, so a maliciously large trailing "subkey" in a stored blob forces PBKDF2 to derive an equally large output. Since `Hash()` always produces exactly `SubkeySize` (32, the SHA-256 digest size) bytes, `Verify` must reject any decoded subkey whose length is not EXACTLY 32 — a strict equality check, not a range.
> **`storedIterations < 1` rejection** (a natural corollary, not a scope expansion): a negative or zero value from a corrupted/adversarial blob would otherwise reach `Rfc2898DeriveBytes.Pbkdf2` and throw an unhandled `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, instead of returning `HashVerificationResult.Failed` the way every other malformed-input case in this method already does.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-86 | Design `CryptographyOptions.MinimumPbkdf2Iterations` (100,000, enforced via the existing `[Range]`/`AddValidatedOptions`/`ValidateOnStart` path) and `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`'s `MaxVerifiableIterations` ceiling (2,000,000, a fixed constant independent of the configured value, checked in `Verify` BEFORE the expensive `Pbkdf2` call) plus the exact-32-byte subkey-length check and the `storedIterations < 1` rejection, exactly per the design above; explicitly record why the ceiling must never be derived from the currently-configured value | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-100 | Implement `CryptographyOptions.MinimumPbkdf2Iterations` + `[Range]` change exactly per D-86 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-101 | Implement `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`'s `MaxVerifiableIterations` ceiling, exact-32-byte subkey-length check, and `storedIterations < 1` rejection exactly per D-86 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| T-79 | Unit tests: `Pbkdf2Iterations` below 100,000 fails `ValidateOnStart`; a crafted hash string with an iteration count above `MaxVerifiableIterations` returns `HashVerificationResult.Failed` WITHOUT ever invoking `Rfc2898DeriveBytes.Pbkdf2` (proven via a call-counting seam or a generous non-flaky elapsed-time upper bound); a crafted hash with a subkey length other than exactly 32 bytes returns `Failed`; a crafted hash with `iterations <= 0` returns `Failed` rather than throwing; an existing hash produced at the current 600,000-iteration default still verifies `Success`; a hash produced at a legitimate below-floor legacy value (e.g. 10,000, simulating a hash stored before this phase shipped) still verifies `Success`/`SuccessRehashNeeded`, never rejected by the new floor | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests | `●` |

## Phase: P-513 — Core: Validate `CryptographicKey.Material` Length Against the Declared Algorithm <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P513 -->

> `CryptographicKey.Material`'s own XML doc already says "32 bytes for AES-256," but nothing enforces it: `new AesGcm(key.Material, TagSize)` accepts any BCL-legal AES key size (16/24/32 bytes) without complaint, silently constructing AES-128-GCM or AES-192-GCM from a shorter/differently-sized key — while every doc, XML comment, and NuGet package description on this platform promises AES-256, with no signal that the real security margin is half (or three-quarters) of what every artifact claims.
> Add `private const int RequiredKeySizeBytes = 32;` to `AesGcmEncryptionService` and a private `EnsureKeySize(CryptographicKey key)` helper, called at the very start of both `EncryptCore` and `DecryptCore` — the two pure cryptographic cores shared by all four sync+async public members per this class's own documented design — BEFORE `new AesGcm(...)` is ever constructed, so the rejection happens strictly before any cryptographic operation is attempted.
> **Throws, does not return a `Result` failure:** a wrong-size key means the registered `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/its backing key material is misconfigured — an infrastructure/provisioning defect, not a runtime/tampered-input condition (which is what `Result<byte[]>` failures in this class are reserved for: tamper, wrong key, unknown KeyId, mismatched AAD). Mirrors `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider.ResolveWrapAlgorithm`'s existing precedent of throwing `NotSupportedException` for an unsupported/misconfigured key type rather than returning a soft failure. Exception type: `CryptographicException`, naming both the expected and actual length — a cryptographic precondition failure, not an argument-shape failure the caller could have avoided (the caller does not control `CryptographicKey.Material`; it comes from `IEncryptionKeyProvider`).
> Applies as an EXACT-LENGTH equality check, not a minimum — a key with too FEW bytes (the headline AES-128-downgrade risk) and too MANY bytes (equally a configuration defect; `AesGcm`'s constructor would otherwise also silently accept 24 bytes/AES-192) are both rejected identically.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-87 | Design the `EnsureKeySize(CryptographicKey)` check point exactly per the design above — a private helper called at the start of both `EncryptCore` and `DecryptCore`, before any `AesGcm` construction, throwing `CryptographicException` naming expected (32) vs. actual length | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-102 | Implement exactly per D-87 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| T-80 | Unit tests: a 16-byte key is rejected by both `Encrypt`/`EncryptAsync` and `Decrypt`/`DecryptAsync` (proving the shared-core check point covers all four public members) with `CryptographicException` naming 32 vs. 16; a 24-byte key is likewise rejected; a 32-byte key is unaffected (existing round-trip tests re-verified green); the rejection is proven to occur before any `AesGcm` instance is constructed / before any partial ciphertext/tag output is produced | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests | `●` |
| DO-43 | XML doc `EnsureKeySize` and both call sites; update `01.Core/README.md`'s Symmetric Encryption section stating the AES-256-only guarantee is now structurally enforced, not merely documented | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| P-46 | Repack `SharedKernel.Cryptography` | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

## Phase: P-514 — Core: Atomic TOTP Replay Guard, Attempt-Throttling Seam, and Config-Consistent Replay Window (BREAKING) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P514 -->

> **The dispatched brief's "zero blast radius" premise is verified FALSE against real, current repository state — corrected here, not silently worked around.** The brief asserts `12.Security.Totp`/P-452 "remains queued, never dispatched" and that `16.Testing` ships no fake for `ITotpReplayGuard`. A repository-wide grep (not a memory-derived guess) finds both claims wrong: `12.Security/SharedKernel.Security.Totp` is a REAL, ALREADY-SHIPPED package (`Challenge/TotpChallengeService.cs`, `Challenge/ITotpChallengeStore.cs`, its own `.Tests` project) — this contradicts the root `CLAUDE.md`'s own "Queued, WO-069/P-452, not yet shipped" line for that domain, which is stale relative to the actual source tree; reconciling that root-brain staleness is outside this file's jurisdiction and is reported to the coordinator, not fixed here. Two REAL implementers of the current two-member `ITotpReplayGuard` shape already exist and will fail to compile once the shape changes: `16.Testing/SharedKernel.Testing/Cryptography/FakeTotpReplayGuard.cs` (a shipped, production `SharedKernel.Testing` fake) and `12.Security.Totp/SharedKernel.Security.Totp.Tests/Challenge/FakeTotpReplayGuard.cs` (an independent, test-local duplicate). **This phase proceeds as BREAKING with a REAL, NON-ZERO migration cost** — two companion migration phases are needed in `16.Testing` and `12.Security`, outside this file's jurisdiction, reported below rather than tracked here.
> **What stays unaffected, and why:** `12.Security.Totp`'s PRODUCTION code (`TotpChallengeService.VerifyAsync`) consumes `TotpVerifier.VerifyAsync(identityKey, secret, code, ct)` by its existing four-argument PUBLIC signature only — it never references `ITotpReplayGuard` directly. As long as every new parameter this phase adds to `TotpVerifier.VerifyAsync` is OPTIONAL and defaults to the exact value the two removed hardcoded constants used, that call site keeps compiling unchanged and keeps producing byte-identical behavior — this is what keeps `12.Security.Totp`'s PRODUCTION source (as opposed to its two test-only fakes) out of the blast radius.
> **The fix — atomic replay marking:** `ITotpReplayGuard`'s two-step `HasBeenUsedAsync`(check)+`MarkUsedAsync`(mark) is replaced by one atomic member, `ValueTask<bool> TryMarkUsedAsync(string identityKey, string code, TimeSpan validityWindow, CancellationToken ct = default)`, returning `true` when THIS call is the first to mark `(identityKey, code)` used (a fresh code, now consumed) and `false` when it was already marked used (a replay) — the same compare-and-set/atomic-reservation shape `18.Idempotency`'s Redis (`SET NX PX`) and EF Core (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) stores already use for the identical class of problem. `TotpVerifier.VerifyAsync` calls `TryMarkUsedAsync` directly after a successful `ValidateCode`, eliminating the check-then-act window entirely — there is no longer a separate "check" step and "mark" step for two concurrent callers to interleave between.
> **The fix — config-consistent replay window:** `TotpVerifier.VerifyAsync` gains new OPTIONAL trailing parameters mirroring `ITotpGenerator.ValidateCode`'s own shape — `int digits = 6, int stepSeconds = 30, int driftWindow = 1, HotpAlgorithm algorithm = HotpAlgorithm.Sha1` (before the existing trailing `ct`) — threaded straight through to `_totpGenerator.ValidateCode(secret, code, digits, stepSeconds, driftWindow, algorithm)`. The replay-window computation (`stepSeconds * ((2 * driftWindow) + 1))`) is computed from these SAME passed-in values, and the two hardcoded `DefaultStepSeconds`/`DefaultDriftWindow` private constants are REMOVED entirely (not merely bypassed) — a caller validating against a non-default step/drift configuration now gets a replay window that actually matches what it validated against.
> **The new attempt-throttling seam — standalone, not wired into `TotpVerifier`:** a new interface, `ITotpAttemptThrottle`, added to the same `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Totp` namespace, mirroring `ITotpReplayGuard`'s own no-default-implementation shape exactly (interface only; XML docs state the consuming service supplies its own store-backed implementation per RFC 4226 §7.3's rate-limiting recommendation). Deliberately NOT added to `TotpVerifier`'s constructor, which stays exactly two parameters (`totpGenerator`, `replayGuard`) — rate-limiting attempts is the caller's own concern (it must decide lockout responses, HTTP 429 shaping, etc.), the same "ships uninvolved, consumer composes" pattern this package already uses for `ITotpReplayGuard` itself and `12.Security.Oidc`'s DPoP replay-check/`ITokenRevocationCheck` seams. This keeps `TotpVerifier`'s constructor signature stable, adding zero further blast radius beyond the `ITotpReplayGuard` shape change itself.
> Depends on: None as a WO-083 phase-ordering matter.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-88 | Design `ITotpReplayGuard`'s atomic `TryMarkUsedAsync` member (replacing `HasBeenUsedAsync`/`MarkUsedAsync`), `TotpVerifier`'s new optional `digits`/`stepSeconds`/`driftWindow`/`algorithm` parameters and actual-parameter-derived replay window (removing the two hardcoded `Default*` constants), and the new standalone `ITotpAttemptThrottle` seam (never wired into `TotpVerifier`'s constructor), exactly per the design above; explicitly correct and record the dispatched brief's "zero blast radius" premise as FALSE, naming both real affected out-of-jurisdiction fakes and confirming `12.Security.Totp`'s production source is unaffected only because of `TotpVerifier.VerifyAsync`'s stable four-argument call shape | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-103 | Implement `ITotpReplayGuard`'s new shape and the new `ITotpAttemptThrottle` interface exactly per D-88 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

| P-47 | Repack `SharedKernel.Cryptography` (BREAKING); release notes state the `ITotpReplayGuard` shape change explicitly and name the two known out-of-domain implementers needing migration | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |

## Phase: P-515 — Core: Fix `SmartEnum` Static-Initialization Trap <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P515 -->

> `SmartEnum<TEnum,TValue>.FromValue`/`TryFromValue`/`FromName` are methods physically declared on the closed generic BASE type. Calling them through a derived enum's name (`Status.FromValue(1)`) resolves, at the CLR level, to `SmartEnum<Status,int>.FromValue` — an INHERITED static member — and per ECMA-335 type-initialization semantics, reaching an inherited static member through a derived type name does NOT guarantee the derived type's (`Status`'s) own static constructor has run first. `Status`'s cctor is exactly what runs its `public static readonly Status Active = new(...)` field initializers, which are what call the protected `SmartEnum(name, value)` instance constructor that adds each member to `_list`. If the very first touch of a `SmartEnum`-derived type is an inherited static call, `_list` can observably be empty at that moment. `List`'s `_readOnlyList ??= _list.AsReadOnly()` self-heals because every instance constructor also does `_readOnlyList = null`, invalidating the cache the next time `List` is read AFTER `Status`'s cctor eventually does run. `_byValue`/`_byName` have no equivalent: they are one-shot `Lazy<Dictionary<...>>` fields — once `.Value` forces `BuildValueDictionary()`/`BuildNameDictionary()` against an empty `_list`, the built (empty) dictionary is cached for the remaining process lifetime with no invalidation hook at all.
> **The fix — force `TEnum`'s cctor exactly once, as part of `SmartEnum<TEnum,TValue>`'s OWN static initialization:** a new private static field on `SmartEnum<TEnum,TValue>` itself — placed after `_list`'s own initializer in declaration order — whose initializer calls `RuntimeHelpers.RunClassConstructor(typeof(TEnum).TypeHandle)`. Because this field is declared on `SmartEnum<TEnum,TValue>` (the same type `_list`/`_byValue`/`_byName`/`List`/`FromValue`/`TryFromValue`/`FromName` are all declared on), the CLR guarantees `SmartEnum<TEnum,TValue>`'s own static constructor — which runs every one of its static field initializers, including this new one — completes before any of those members are ever accessible to a caller. This forces `TEnum`'s cctor (and therefore every member's registration into `_list`) to have already happened by the time `_byValue`/`_byName`'s `Lazy<>` factories could possibly run, closing the gap structurally rather than by re-checking on every call.
> **Zero hot-path cost:** the force-call happens exactly ONCE per closed generic type (`SmartEnum<Status,int>`, `SmartEnum<AnotherEnum,string>`, etc.), during that type's own one-time static initialization — never inside `FromValue`/`TryFromValue`/`FromName` themselves, which stay untouched.
> **AOT/trim note:** `typeof(TEnum)` here is a directly-expressed reference to a generic type parameter, substituted with a concrete, statically-known type at every call site by the JIT/AOT compiler — a pattern the ILLink trimmer and NativeAOT analyzer already recognize and preserve correctly (this is not a dynamically-computed `Type`, and `RunClassConstructor` itself performs no reflection-based member enumeration — it only runs a type's initializer). No `[DynamicallyAccessedMembers]` annotation is needed for this specific intrinsic; if the trimmer/AOT analyzer nonetheless emits a warning for this call shape when actually built and tested, resolve it via a narrowly-scoped `[UnconditionalSuppressMessage]` with a justification comment — never a blanket suppression.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-89 | Design the fix exactly per the design above — the new force-initializer field on `SmartEnum<TEnum,TValue>`, its placement relative to `_list`, and the AOT/trim rationale for `typeof(TEnum)` needing no `[DynamicallyAccessedMembers]` annotation | SharedKernel.Primitives | `●` |
| C-105 | Implement the fix exactly per D-89 | SharedKernel.Primitives | `●` |
| T-83 | Test touching a derived `SmartEnum` EXCLUSIVELY through inherited static members (`FromValue`/`TryFromValue`/`FromName`/`List`, never referencing one of its own named static instances first) proves all four resolve correctly — the exact scenario that fails against the pre-fix code | SharedKernel.Primitives.Tests | `●` |
| DO-45 | XML docs on the new field explaining the trap and the fix; `01.Core/README.md`'s `SmartEnum` section gains a short note confirming the fix adds zero cost to the hot lookup path (the force-call runs once per closed generic type, never per call) | SharedKernel.Primitives | `●` |
| P-48 | Repack `SharedKernel.Primitives` to the local feed | SharedKernel.Primitives | `●` |

## Phase: P-516 — Core: Thread-Safe, Freezable `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog` <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P516 -->

> `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog` wraps a plain, unsynchronized `Dictionary<(string,string),string>` behind a public `AddTranslation` mutator, registered as a process-lifetime singleton — a data race the moment any code calls `AddTranslation` concurrently with a reader. Confirmed against the real shipped construction path (`AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog`'s DI factory): every `AddTranslation` call today already happens synchronously, inside the `configure` callback, fully before the singleton is ever handed to any consumer — a genuine `ConcurrentDictionary`/lock rewrite would add real overhead to the read-hot-path of a "zero-config default" type to defend a mutation window that, on the one shipped construction path, never actually overlaps a reader.
> **The fix — freeze, not lock:** an additive `Seal()` method (idempotent) and `IsSealed` property on `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog`. `AddTranslation` checks a `volatile`/`Interlocked`-backed sealed flag first and throws `InvalidOperationException` with a clear message once sealed. `AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog`'s DI factory calls `catalog.Seal()` immediately after `configure?.Invoke(catalog)` returns, BEFORE returning the instance — the common, shipped DI path becomes safe with ZERO consumer action required. A consumer who constructs `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog` directly (outside this package's DI extension) and genuinely wants runtime-mutable translations simply never calls `Seal()` — but is then, by design, on their own for concurrency discipline; the documented, sanctioned answer for a fully dynamic runtime-mutable catalog is to implement `ILocalizationCatalog` directly rather than lean on this type's deliberately seed-once shape.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-90 | Design `Seal()`/`IsSealed` on `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog` and the auto-seal wiring inside `AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog`'s DI factory, exactly per the design above | SharedKernel.Localization | `●` |
| C-106 | Implement per D-90 | SharedKernel.Localization | `●` |
| T-84 | Tests: (a) `AddTranslation` after `Seal()` always throws `InvalidOperationException`, never corrupts dictionary state; (b) concurrent `TryGetString` reads against a sealed catalog never throw or return corrupted results; (c) the `ILocalizationCatalog` resolved from `AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog`'s DI registration is already sealed — a subsequent `AddTranslation` call (via the concrete type) throws | SharedKernel.Localization.Tests | `●` |
| DO-46 | XML docs on `Seal()`/`IsSealed`/`AddTranslation`'s new throw behavior explaining the seed-once-then-immutable lifecycle decision and why (thread safety with zero lock overhead on the read path); explicit note that `StringLocalizerLocalizationCatalog` is unaffected (no equivalent mutator) | SharedKernel.Localization | `●` |

## Phase: P-518 — Core: Standardize DI Registration Idiom to `TryAdd`/`TryAddEnumerable`; Fix Double-Registration <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P518 -->

> **Real, grep-verified blast radius (not inherited from the dispatched brief's estimate):** 3 registrations already use `TryAddSingleton` today — `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2`'s `ISecureRandomGenerator`, `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s `ISecureRandomGenerator`, and `SharedKernel.Validation`'s `INationalIdValidatorRegistry` — against roughly 27 plain `AddSingleton`/`AddKeyedSingleton` calls: `SharedKernel.Cryptography`'s `AddSharedKernelCryptography` (9 `AddSingleton` + 2 `AddKeyedSingleton` = 11), `SharedKernel.Compression`'s `AddSharedKernelCompression` (1 `AddSingleton` + 2 `AddKeyedSingleton` = 3), `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2`'s `AddSharedKernelArgon2Cryptography` (1 remaining `AddKeyedSingleton`), `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` (7 `AddSingleton` + 1 stray `AddSingleton<IValidateOptions<...>,...>` outside the `AddValidatedOptions` chain = 8), `SharedKernel.FeatureManagement`'s `AddSharedKernelFeatureManagement` (this domain's own `services.AddSingleton<IFeatureManager,...>()` line only — never the preceding third-party `MsftFeatureManagement.ServiceCollectionExtensions.AddFeatureManagement(...)` call it sits beside, which is out of this domain's control), `SharedKernel.Localization`'s two `AddSingleton<ILocalizationCatalog>(...)` factory registrations, and `SharedKernel.Primitives`' `ClockExtensions.AddClock()`. This confirms the dispatched brief's headline claim exactly: `AddSharedKernelCryptography()` called twice really does double-register all eleven of its services today.
> **A call site the dispatched brief did not distinguish, and converting it the naive way would be a REGRESSION — caught during design, not left to the implementer to discover the hard way:** `SharedKernel.Validation`'s `AddNationalIdValidator<TValidator>()` registers `INationalIdValidator` as a genuine, INTENTIONAL multi-implementation collection — `NationalIdValidatorRegistry` is built by iterating `sp.GetServices<INationalIdValidator>()` inside `AddSharedKernelValidation`'s own (already-correct) `TryAddSingleton<INationalIdValidatorRegistry>` factory. Converting `AddNationalIdValidator<TValidator>()`'s `services.AddSingleton<INationalIdValidator, TValidator>()` to a plain `TryAddSingleton` would silently drop every country validator registered after the FIRST one ever registered — a severe correctness regression this fix must not introduce. The correct idiom there is `services.TryAddEnumerable(ServiceDescriptor.Singleton<INationalIdValidator, TValidator>())`, which still prevents the identical `(INationalIdValidator, TValidator)` pair from double-registering (the actual double-registration bug class this phase targets) while preserving the collection's multi-country semantics untouched.
> **A second, smaller wrinkle, also caught during design:** `SharedKernel.Localization`'s `AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog`/`AddStringLocalizerCatalog<TResource>` are documented today as "mutually exclusive — whichever call runs LAST wins" (standard plain-`AddSingleton` last-registration-wins behavior). Converting both to `TryAddSingleton` deliberately and KNOWINGLY inverts this to first-call-wins — an intentional, acceptance-criteria-authorized side effect of standardizing the whole domain onto one idiom (every DI extension method across the domain must use `TryAdd*`/`TryAddEnumerable` consistently), not an oversight. Both methods' XML docs must be updated to describe the new behavior, not merely the code.
> Depends on: None. **Unblocks `00.Governance`'s `P-523`**, which mechanically locks this exact convention and depends on this phase shipping first.
> WO-083.

## Phase: P-519 — Core: Additive Source-Generator-Based Options-Validation Path <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P519 -->

> **Precedent already exists ad hoc inside this domain:** `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` already calls `services.AddSingleton<IValidateOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions>, AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptionsValidator>()` — a caller-supplied `IValidateOptions<T>` used alongside `AddValidatedOptions`. This phase promotes that pattern into a first-class, reusable `SharedKernel.Configuration` entry point rather than leaving every future consumer to hand-roll it.
> **The fix:** an additive `AddValidatedOptions<TOptions, TValidator>(this IServiceCollection, IConfigurationSection)` overload in `OptionsExtensions`, where `TValidator : class, IValidateOptions<TOptions>` — binds `TOptions` to the section (deliberately calling NO `.ValidateDataAnnotations()`, since the entire point is avoiding that reflection-based validator), registers `TValidator` via `services.TryAddSingleton<IValidateOptions<TOptions>, TValidator>()` (using the `TryAdd` idiom from day one, consistent with `P-518`'s domain-wide standardization even though this is new code with no prior double-registration history to fix), and calls `.ValidateOnStart()` identically to the existing overload. `TValidator` is expected to be a caller-authored `[OptionsValidator]`-generated partial class (the in-box BCL source generator) or any other hand-written `IValidateOptions<T>` — this package does not require or reference the generator itself, only the resulting interface.
> The existing `AddValidatedOptions<TOptions>(section)` (DataAnnotations) overload is completely unchanged and remains the default — every existing consumer across the platform keeps working unmodified.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

| C-115 | Implement per D-93 | SharedKernel.Configuration | `●` |

## Phase: P-521 — Core: Versioned, Dated Reference Tables with an Opt-In Fallback Mode <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P521 -->

> `IbanValidator`'s hardcoded 78-entry per-country length table (confirmed exact count against real source) and the `IsoCurrencyValidator`/`IsoCountryValidator` ISO 4217/ISO 3166 data each gain a documented as-of/registry-version public constant. `IbanValidator` gains a new optional trailing parameter, `Validate(string? value, bool allowFallbackForUnknownCountry = false)` (mirrored on `IsValid`) — default `false` preserves today's exact behavior (an unrecognized country prefix hard-rejects); when explicitly `true`, an unrecognized country prefix skips the per-country length check and instead applies the ISO 13616 mod-97 check-digit algorithm alone (still requiring the general IBAN shape: two-letter alpha prefix, two check digits, alphanumeric BBAN, overall length within the general ISO 13616 bound) — trading country-specific length/structure checking for acceptance of a country the table does not yet know about.
> **Explicitly out of scope for this phase, recorded rather than silently dropped:** a matching `.MustBeValidIban(allowFallbackForUnknownCountry:)` overload on `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation`'s rule-builder adapter — not named in this phase's own acceptance criteria; left as a natural, small follow-on if a consumer ever needs the fallback specifically via that adapter.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

## Phase: P-522 — Core: Bound the Compiled-Regex Cache Used by Format/Email Guards <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P522 -->

> **Confirmed against real source:** the `ConcurrentDictionary<string, Regex>` (`_regexCache`) backing `Guard.Against.InvalidFormat`/`.Email` still lives in `SharedKernel.Guards/Clauses/GuardClauseExtensions.cs` today — `P-505` (the `SharedKernel.Guards` → `SharedKernel.Core` merge, `SharedKernel.Guards` namespace preserved) has not yet shipped. This phase targets wherever `P-505` lands that code (`SharedKernel.Core`, under the `SharedKernel.Guards` namespace) — it cannot start before `P-505` ships.
> **The fix:** a bounded, approximately-FIFO-evicting cache replacing the unbounded dictionary — a fixed internal capacity constant (e.g. 256 distinct patterns, chosen so every current, compile-time-literal call site fits with enormous headroom and is never observably affected) plus a `ConcurrentQueue<string>` recording insertion order for genuinely NEW entries only (enqueued inside the `GetOrAdd` value-factory, which runs only on a cache miss); when the dictionary's count exceeds the cap after an insert, dequeue and `TryRemove` oldest entries until back at/under the cap. This is honestly documented as an approximate/soft bound under heavy concurrent churn (a lightweight, BCL-only mechanism — deliberately not a strict LRU and not a new caching-library dependency), sufficient for the currently-zero-risk, future-proofing purpose this phase exists for: every existing call site passes a literal pattern known at compile time, so the cache today never approaches even a small fraction of the cap; the bound exists purely against a future call site that might ever derive a pattern from configuration or user input.
> Depends on: `P-505` (`SharedKernel.Guards` → `SharedKernel.Core` merge must ship first).
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|

| C-117 | Implement per D-96, wherever P-505 lands the Guards surface | SharedKernel.Core (post-P-505; `SharedKernel.Guards` namespace) | `●` |
| T-91 | Test presenting more than the capacity constant's worth of distinct dynamically-constructed patterns through the guard extension (via an internal test seam or by driving `Guard.Against.InvalidFormat`/`.Email` with many unique patterns) and asserting the cache's size never exceeds its documented bound after the workload completes; a second test confirming every existing literal-pattern call site is unaffected (still resolves the identical compiled `Regex` instance across repeated calls with the same pattern) | SharedKernel.Core.Tests | `●` |

## Phase: P-524 — Core: Zero Internally-Owned Key Material After Use <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P524 -->

> **Ratified scope — narrowed deliberately, do not reopen:** this phase ACCEPTS zeroization of buffers this package genuinely owns and controls the lifetime of, and DECLINES two broader redesigns already ruled on: an `IDisposable CryptographicKey` (a use-after-dispose hazard against `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`'s shared, multi-reader, process-lifetime cache — the point a key's lifetime genuinely ends is the CACHE's own eviction, not any individual caller's read) and `ReadOnlySpan<char>`/`char[]` secret overloads on `IOneWayHasher` (the realistic caller — a request DTO field, an `IConfiguration` value — already holds the secret as an immutable, already-allocated `string` by the time it reaches this API; a `Span` overload cannot un-happen that upstream allocation).
> **The precise, source-verified target set — correcting the dispatched brief's own ambiguous framing:** `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`'s `salt`/`subkey` (`Hash`) and `actualSubkey`/`expectedSubkey`/`salt` (`Verify`, after the `CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals` comparison); `Argon2idOneWayHasher`'s identical five-buffer shape (`secretBytes`/`salt`/`subkey`/`actualSubkey`/`expectedSubkey`); `HmacSha256Signer.Verify`'s internal `expected` comparison buffer (`Sign`'s own return value IS the caller's needed output and must never be zeroed). The brief named "decrypted plaintext scratch buffers inside `AesGcmEncryptionService`" — verified against real source, the ONLY buffers matching that description that this package genuinely owns AND never hands back to a caller are the intermediate `byte[]` conversions INSIDE `EncryptToString(Async)`/`DecryptToString(Async)`'s STRING overloads (`Encoding.UTF8.GetBytes(plaintext)` before encrypting; the decrypted `byte[]` immediately after `Encoding.UTF8.GetString(...)` has already extracted its content into the returned string). **Explicitly, and this distinction is load-bearing:** the `byte[]` returned DIRECTLY to a caller by `Encrypt`/`EncryptAsync`/`Decrypt`/`DecryptAsync`'s primary `byte[]`-based overloads is NEVER zeroed by this phase — it is the caller's own needed output that they still need to read after the call returns; zeroing it would silently corrupt the very result the caller asked for, a new bug this design explicitly avoids introducing.
> Depends on: None (targets `Argon2idOneWayHasher` too — `P-495` has already shipped, so the dispatched brief's "(once P-495 ships)" qualifier is moot).
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-97 | Design the precise, narrowed buffer target set exactly per the design above, explicitly recording why the primary `Encrypt`/`Decrypt`/`EncryptAsync`/`DecryptAsync` `byte[]` return values are OUT of scope | SharedKernel.Cryptography, SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2 | `●` |
| C-118 | Implement the `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`/`HmacSha256Signer`/`AesGcmEncryptionService` (String overloads only) zeroization exactly per D-97 | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| C-119 | Implement the `Argon2idOneWayHasher` zeroization exactly per D-97 | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2 | `●` |
| T-92 | A test using an internal, test-only seam (gated via `[InternalsVisibleTo]` to each package's own `.Tests` project — never a change to the public surface) proves a representative package-owned buffer (e.g. `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`'s `subkey`, or `AesGcmEncryptionService`'s `EncryptToString` intermediate) is genuinely all-zero bytes immediately after the public call returns — not merely that the code compiles or references `CryptographicOperations.ZeroMemory` | SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests, SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2.Tests | `●` |
| DO-53 | `SharedKernel.Cryptography/README.md` gains a "Key-Material Zeroization" section documenting exactly which buffers are zeroed, the two DECLINED broader asks (no `IDisposable CryptographicKey`; no `ReadOnlySpan<char>`/`char[]` `IOneWayHasher` overloads) and why, plus upstream guidance for a consumer wanting to minimize a secret string's lifetime before it ever reaches this package | SharedKernel.Cryptography | `●` |
| P-56 | Repack `SharedKernel.Cryptography` and `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2` to the local feed | SharedKernel.Cryptography, SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2 | `●` |

## Phase: P-525 — Core: LEI, ABA Routing Number, and SEPA Creditor Identifier Validators <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P525 -->

> Three new format validators in `SharedKernel.Validation`, each following the exact shape every existing validator in this package already uses — dual-mode standalone `Result`/bool + `Guard.Against.*` extension, a new nested `ValidationErrorCodes` class per this package's existing per-validator-nested-class convention (never added to `SharedKernel.Primitives.ErrorCodes`), zero FluentValidation dependency in the core package: **`LeiValidator`** (ISO 17442 — a 20-character alphanumeric identifier: 4-character LOU prefix + 14-character entity-specific reference + 2 numeric check digits, validated via the same mod-97-10/letters-map-to-two-digit-numerics approach `IbanValidator` already uses); **`AbaRoutingNumberValidator`** (a 9-digit US bank routing number, validated via the published `(3,7,1)` repeating-weight checksum, sum ≡ 0 mod 10); **`SepaCreditorIdentifierValidator`** (2-letter ISO 3166 country code + 2 check digits validated via an `IbanValidator`-style rearrange-and-mod-97 approach + a 3-character creditor business code (or the literal `"ZZZ"`) + up to 28-character national creditor identifier, overall length ≤ 35 per the published SEPA Creditor Identifier structure).
> **No architectural gap — pure catalog completeness.** This is the identical shape (a published checksum algorithm over a structured identifier) already living in this exact package; the domain simply had not yet needed these three. **Test-vector discipline, unchanged from this package's own `P-443` precedent:** every test vector must be sourced from a real published reference (the ISO 17442 registry's own published examples, a published ABA routing-number checksum reference, a published SEPA creditor-identifier worked example) at implementation time — never hand-constructed. This design phase does not itself hardcode vector values.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

## Phase: P-526 — Core: FIPS 140-3 / Approved-Algorithm Posture Statement (docs-only) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P526 -->

> A dedicated FIPS 140-3 posture statement — zero code or public-API change, verified by confirming no `.cs` file changes anywhere in this phase's diff. Enumerates, per shipped primitive: **FIPS-approved** — PBKDF2-HMACSHA256 (`Pbkdf2OneWayHasher`, SP 800-132), AES-256-GCM (`AesGcmEncryptionService`, SP 800-38D), RSA and ECDSA at their documented minimum key sizes (`RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService`, 2048-bit / 256-bit NIST curves, FIPS 186), HMACSHA256 (`HmacSha256Signer`, FIPS 198-1), SHA-256 (`Sha256ContentHasher`/`IContentHasher`, FIPS 180-4). **NOT FIPS-approved** — Argon2id (`Argon2idOneWayHasher`; no FIPS 140-3 validated status exists for Argon2 as of this posture statement). **The gap this phase exists specifically to surface** — the RFC 4226/6238 DEFAULT `HotpAlgorithm.Sha1` (`HotpGenerator`/`TotpGenerator`/`TotpVerifier`, used unless a caller explicitly selects a different algorithm): SHA-1 carries restricted/deprecated status for this purpose under FIPS-enforced mode, so a FIPS-constrained consumer must explicitly pass `HotpAlgorithm.Sha256` or `.Sha512` to every call rather than relying on the RFC-default parameter value.
> Explicit, actionable guidance for a service operating under a FIPS-enforced-mode compliance requirement: use PBKDF2 (never Argon2id), and explicitly pass `HotpAlgorithm.Sha256`/`.Sha512` to every TOTP/HOTP call.
> Depends on: None.
> WO-083.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|

## Phase: P-529 — Core: `SharedKernel.Primitives` Pre-First-Publish Hardening Pass (BEHAVIOUR CHANGES) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P529 -->

> **Origin, recorded honestly:** user-directed, not dispatched by `arch-lead`. `SharedKernel.Primitives` was selected as the first `01.Core` package to actually publish to GitHub Packages (the three `00.Governance` packages went out first, at floating MinVer heights `1.0.0-alpha.0.882`/`.884`/`.886`), and this phase is the gold-standard audit that preceded it. Every finding below was produced by EXECUTING the compiled assembly and running the trim/AOT analyzers over it — not by reading source. Four of the six defects directly contradicted the affected type's own XML documentation, which is why reading would not have found them.
> **Why the behaviour changes were taken now rather than deferred:** nothing in `01.Core` has ever been pushed to a feed, so every one of these was free at the time of this phase. Under MinVer lockstep each would otherwise have become a MAJOR bump across all 67 packages. This window does not reopen.
> **DEFECTS (all six verified by execution, each pinned by a test proven to fail when the fix is reverted — perturbation results recorded per task):**
>
> 1. `Result.Error` returned `null` for an uninitialized `default(Result)`. The struct's all-zero value reports `IsFailure == true` while holding no error, so `Error` returned `null` in violation of `Error`'s own documented "never null" contract, surfacing as a `NullReferenceException` at whatever call site next touched `result.Error.Code` — typically `Error.ToProblemDetails()` at the HTTP boundary, arbitrarily far from the code that produced it. Reachable without anyone writing `default`: the `out` parameter of a failed `Dictionary.TryGetValue`, an element of `new Result[n]`, an unassigned field. `Result`'s own type-level XML doc asserted the opposite — that the zero-value problem did not apply to it.
> 2. `ValidationResult`/`ValidationResult<T>` equality was broken ASYMMETRICALLY. Both are declared `record`, but the `IReadOnlyList<Error>` member made the compiler-generated `Equals` fall back to reference equality: two failures built from equal errors compared UNEQUAL, while two successes compared EQUAL (both reuse the one static empty array). Equality that holds or breaks depending on the outcome is worse than uniformly absent equality, because a passing success-path test implies nothing about the failure path.
> 3. `ValidationResult`/`ValidationResult<T>` were not immutable. `Failure` stored the caller's own list by reference — `IReadOnlyList<T>` is a read-only VIEW, not an immutable collection, and a `List<T>` satisfies it — so a caller mutating it afterwards mutated the record. Clearing it produced a failed result carrying ZERO errors: precisely the state `Failure`'s own `ArgumentException` guard rejects at construction, reached by going around the guard instead of through it.
> 4. The package advertised AOT compatibility it did not have. `PackageTags` carried `aot` and `<Description>` said "AOT-compatible", while `SmartEnum.ForceEnumStaticConstructor` emitted a real `IL2059` trim warning into EVERY trimming or AOT-compiling consumer's build — a hard failure under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, pointing into this package rather than theirs. The `_forceEnumStaticConstructor` XML doc (written by P-515) explicitly asserted "No `[DynamicallyAccessedMembers]` annotation is needed for this specific intrinsic." A self-contained `TrimMode=full` publish confirmed the MECHANISM is nonetheless correct, so the resolution is a justified suppression plus a doc that states what was measured, not a redesign.
> 5. `SmartEnum.TryFromValue(null)` THREW `ArgumentNullException` (with `Parameter: key`, leaked from `Dictionary` internals) instead of returning `false` — a contract violation for a `Try`-shaped member, reachable whenever `TValue` is a reference type such as `string`.
> 6. `Result.Failure`/`Result<T>.Failure` and both implicit `Error` conversions accepted a `null` error unchecked, constructing defect 1 deliberately. `ValidationResult.Failure` likewise accepted a `null` element.
> **ADDITIONS:** `ErrorCodes.Forbidden` (`Default`, `InsufficientPermission`), closing the gap left when `ErrorType.Forbidden` shipped in P-384/WO-059 without companion codes, unlike `BusinessRule`/`Domain.RuleViolated`; `WellKnownBaggageKeys.TenantId`; `IComparable<TEnum>`/`IComparable` on `SmartEnum`; `SmartEnumJsonConverter<TEnum, TValue>`; `SmartEnum.TryFromName`; `[DebuggerDisplay]` across `Result`/`Result<T>`/`Error`/`ValidationResult`/`ValidationResult<T>`/`SmartEnum`.
> **TWO DESIGN DECISIONS THAT WENT AGAINST THE FIRST INSTINCT, both caught by measurement rather than review — record these, they are the reusable lessons:**
> **(a) `IComparable<TEnum>` does NOT widen `SmartEnum`'s `TValue` constraint.** The obvious `where TValue : IEquatable<TValue>, IComparable<TValue>` compiles inside this package and passed a survey of every concrete `SmartEnum` subclass in the repo (all use `int`). It then broke the full-solution build with two `CS0314` errors, because `SharedKernel.Core`'s `Guard.Against.InvalidSmartEnum<TEnum, TValue>` is ITSELF generic over `TValue` and constrains only `IEquatable` — so the constraint would have had to widen on another package's public API, turning an additive change into a cross-package breaking one. Comparison goes through `Comparer<TValue>.Default` instead. Trade-off accepted and documented on the type: a genuinely unordered `TValue` fails on first compare rather than at compile time. **The generalizable lesson: surveying concrete subclasses is not sufficient to assess a constraint change — generic forwarders in other packages must be searched for too.**
> **(b) `SmartEnumJsonConverter` must use the `JsonTypeInfo<T>` overloads, never the obvious ones.** `JsonSerializer.Deserialize<T>(ref reader, options)`, `JsonSerializer.Serialize(writer, value, options)`, and `JsonSerializerOptions.GetConverter(Type)` are ALL annotated `[RequiresUnreferencedCode]` + `[RequiresDynamicCode]`, each measured emitting `IL2026` plus `IL3050` from this file — the converter's own first draft would have introduced the exact defect class this phase was correcting (4), in the same commit, under an XML doc claiming it "performs no reflection… survives full trimming". Only `JsonSerializerOptions.GetTypeInfo(Type)` and the `JsonTypeInfo<T>` overloads are unannotated. **Generalizable lesson: run `EnableTrimAnalyzer`+`EnableAotAnalyzer` before writing any AOT claim into a doc comment.**
> **ALSO CORRECTED:** `GenerateDocumentationFile` set per-project — no package in this repo shipped a `.xml` because the SDK assigns it `false` early enough that `Directory.Build.targets`' empty-string-guarded default never fires, so the nupkg previously contained the assembly and no docs. `README.md` rewritten (38 → ~250 lines): its "Zero external dependencies" claim was false (the same defect class P-517 corrected in `<Description>`, which this phase found had survived in the README), and it documented 6 of 16 public types. Dead `using System.Collections.Concurrent` removed from `SmartEnum.cs`. `SmartEnum` duplicate-member and unknown-member diagnostics now name the type, the key, and both colliding members instead of leaking `"An item with the same key has already been added. Key: 1"` from inside a `Lazy`.
> **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** no metadata bag on `Error` (standing `### DECLINED` ruling, not reopened); `13.ServiceDefaults`' local `TenantBaggageKeys.TenantId` NOT re-pointed at the new registry constant (out of the scope set for this pass — but because the registry value was chosen as `"TenantId"` rather than `"tenant.id"`, that re-point is now a behaviour-identical refactor whenever it is taken); no null guards on `Error`'s own `Code`/`Message` (considered, left — a positional record would need a body, and a null code produces bad data rather than a crash).
> Depends on: None.
> User-directed pre-publish hardening.

## Phase: P-530 — Core: `SharedKernel.Configuration` Pre-Publish Hardening Pass (BEHAVIOUR CHANGES) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P530 -->

> **Origin, recorded honestly:** user-directed, not dispatched by `arch-lead`. `SharedKernel.Configuration` was chosen as the next package after `SharedKernel.Primitives` (P-529) on measured grounds: of the wave-2 packages that depend on Primitives alone, it has the most dependents repo-wide (23 referencing projects, against `SharedKernel.Core`'s 11), and it gates the whole `SharedKernel.Cryptography` sub-tree — `SharedKernel.Cryptography` (14 dependents), `.Compression`, `.Cryptography.Argon2`, and `.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` all reference it. It is also the cheapest audit in the domain: one source file, 139 lines, two public methods before this pass.
> **DEFECTS (all verified by execution, each pinned by a test proven to fail when the fix is reverted):**
>
> 1. **`AddValidatedOptions<TOptions, TValidator>` silently registered nothing.** It used `TryAddSingleton<IValidateOptions<TOptions>, TValidator>()`. `IValidateOptions<T>` is a multi-implementation COLLECTION — the options pipeline runs every registered validator for a type, never just the first — so `TryAdd` found the service type already claimed and skipped. Measured: registering the Data Annotations overload and then the validator overload for one options type left the caller's cross-property validator unregistered, and configuration violating it (`MinSize=100, MaxSize=1`) started the host cleanly. **This is the exact regression class this domain's own `01.Core/README.md` already documents as named `TryAdd` exception #2**, and `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` hand-rolls `TryAddEnumerable(...)` immediately after its own `AddValidatedOptions` call specifically to route around it (`AzureKeyVaultCryptographyServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:86-89`). The convention was written down after P-518 found it downstream; the package that CAUSES it was never fixed. The pre-existing test `AddValidatedOptions_WithGeneratedValidator_CalledTwice_RegistersValidatorOnlyOnce` asserted the broken behaviour as though it were the intended contract.
> 2. **Two distinct validators for one options type: the second was dropped.** Same root cause as (1); recorded separately because it is the half a reader would not predict from (1) alone — a consumer composing two independent rule sets loses one silently.
> 3. **The package advertised AOT compatibility it did not have.** `PackageTags` carried `aot` and the README's own heading read "Source-Generated Options Validation (opt-in, **AOT-clean**)". Measured under `EnableTrimAnalyzer`+`EnableAotAnalyzer`: **6× `IL2091`, 4× `IL2026`, 2× `IL3050`** injected into every trimming consumer's build. `OptionsBuilder<TOptions>.Bind` is annotated `[RequiresUnreferencedCode]` AND `[RequiresDynamicCode]` and is called by BOTH overloads — so the source-generated path makes *validation* reflection-free but never *binding*, and the README claim was false for the one overload it was written about. Same defect class as P-529's `IL2059`.
> 4. **Not idempotent.** The BCL's `ValidateDataAnnotations()` registers `DataAnnotationValidateOptions<T>` with a plain `AddSingleton`, so calling the overload twice for one options type validated twice: measured **4 duplicated failure messages instead of 2** in `OptionsValidationException.Failures`. Violates the domain-wide `TryAdd` convention P-518 established.
> 5. **Dead dependency.** The nuspec forced `SharedKernel.Primitives` on every consumer while the package's entire source referenced no `SharedKernel` type at all — verified by grep over the single source file, which uses only `Microsoft.Extensions.*`. The `<Description>` advertised the dependency as though it were load-bearing.
> 6. **No XML documentation shipped.** Same per-project `GenerateDocumentationFile` cause as P-529/C-127; confirmed by inspecting the packed nupkg, which contained the assembly and no `.xml`.
> **TWO UNDOCUMENTED BEHAVIOURS, both measured and now stated in the XML docs and the README:** (a) `ValidateOnStart()` is inert without a real host — with a bare `ServiceCollection` and `BuildServiceProvider()`, invalid configuration resolves fine and throws only on first `.Value` read, so the fail-fast guarantee belongs to `IHost.StartAsync()` and not to this package, which the README previously promised unconditionally; (b) once anything resolves `IOptionsMonitor<T>`, a reload introducing an invalid value throws `AggregateException(OptionsValidationException)` **out of `IConfigurationRoot.Reload()` itself** — under `reloadOnChange: true` that lands on the file-watcher's thread with no caller waiting to catch it. With no monitor resolved, `Reload()` is silent and the failure defers to the next read.
> **ADDITIONS:** `ISectionBoundOptions` — a `static abstract string SectionName { get; }` contract plus two `IConfiguration`-taking overloads that read it, so the section path is declared on the options type and no call site can pass the wrong one; named-options support (`string? name` on all four overloads); a `validateDataAnnotations` flag composing Data Annotations with a custom validator, which defect (1) made impossible.
> **ONE DESIGN DECISION THAT WENT AGAINST THE FIRST INSTINCT, caught by measurement — record it, it generalizes:** the obvious fix for defect (4) is `TryAddEnumerable`, matching what P-518 prescribed everywhere else. Measured, that would have introduced a NEW defect: `TryAddEnumerable` de-duplicates on IMPLEMENTATION TYPE, and every named options instance shares the one implementation type `DataAnnotationValidateOptions<TOptions>`, while that validator is itself name-scoped and SKIPS other names. So it would have registered the validator for the first name registered and left every other named instance of that type completely unvalidated — starting a host cleanly on invalid configuration, the same shape as defect (1). The fix is a per-NAME duplicate check over `ServiceDescriptor.ImplementationInstance`, which is possible only because the validator is registered as a pre-built immutable instance rather than via a factory (a factory descriptor exposes no inspectable name). Pinned in both directions: perturbing to a name-blind `TryAddEnumerable` fails exactly the two tests that cover it. **Generalizable lesson: `TryAddEnumerable` is not a drop-in for `TryAddSingleton` when the service is keyed on something finer than its implementation type.**
> **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s hand-rolled `TryAddEnumerable` workaround NOT collapsed onto the now-working combined overload — it is correct as written and belongs to that package's own scope, though it is now expressible as `AddValidatedOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions, AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptionsValidator>(configuration, validateDataAnnotations: true)`; the 33 existing options types across 12 domains NOT retrofitted onto `ISectionBoundOptions` (opt-in and non-breaking by design — adoption is each domain's own call, and it requires changing `public const string SectionName` to `public static string SectionName =>` since a const field cannot satisfy a `static abstract` property); no `IConfiguration` wrapper, no secrets provider, no FluentValidation adapter.
> Depends on: None.
> User-directed pre-publish hardening.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|

| C-129 | Data Annotations validation registered by hand as an immutable `DataAnnotationValidateOptions<TOptions>` instance behind a per-NAME duplicate check, replacing the BCL's `ValidateDataAnnotations()` — idempotent per `(TOptions, name)` without collapsing distinct named instances onto one validator. See the design note above | SharedKernel.Configuration | `●` |
| C-130 | `ISectionBoundOptions` with `static abstract string SectionName { get; }`, plus `AddValidatedOptions<TOptions>(IConfiguration)` and `AddValidatedOptions<TOptions, TValidator>(IConfiguration)` reading it — zero reflection, resolved as a direct static call through the generic type parameter. An explicit `IConfigurationSection` argument still wins overload resolution, so a type can be pointed at a different section | SharedKernel.Configuration | `●` |

Defaults to `false` because an `[OptionsValidator]`-generated validator already evaluates those attributes and enabling both double-reports every attribute failure | SharedKernel.Configuration | `●` |

| V-02 | Whole-solution verification: `Platform.SharedKernel.slnx -c Release` → 0 errors and no new warnings at any of the 27 in-repo `AddValidatedOptions` call sites; `Platform.SharedKernel.Unit.slnf` → 5,310 tests across 49 projects, 0 failures; `EnableTrimAnalyzer`+`EnableAotAnalyzer` → 0 IL warnings; packed nupkg ships `lib/net10.0/SharedKernel.Configuration.xml` (10 documented members) with zero `SharedKernel.*` dependencies | SharedKernel (domain-wide) | `●` |

## Phase: P-538 — Core: `SharedKernel.Core` Pre-First-Publish Hardening Pass (BREAKING API + BEHAVIOUR CHANGES) <!-- phase-key: SK.01.P538 -->

> **Origin, recorded honestly:** user-directed, not dispatched by `arch-lead`. `SharedKernel.Core` is next in the publish chain after `SharedKernel.Primitives` (P-529), its only dependency. Nothing in the package had been pushed to a feed, so the user explicitly took the breaking window: every API change below was free now and would be a MAJOR bump across all 80 lockstep packages later. The user ruled on four design points: `Guard.Throw` keeps throwing `DomainException`; `Guard.Against` keeps returning `Error?`, with bridges added; the exception subclass stays permissive, with `error.ToException()` added; `.Clauses` is folded into `SharedKernel.Guards` and the BCL duplicates are deleted.
> **DEFECTS (all verified by execution in a probe app before the fix, each pinned by a test):**
>
> 1. **The async railway lost exceptions and cancellation.** Every `Task<Result<T>>` overload used `ContinueWith(t => t.Result.X(...))`. A faulted source surfaced as `AggregateException(InvalidOperationException)` instead of the original exception, and a cancelled source as `AggregateException` instead of `OperationCanceledException`, contradicting the cancellation rule `ResultTry` already enforces (P-510). A continuation returning a null `Task` surfaced as a misleading `TaskCanceledException`.
> 2. **Functional guards threw.** `ShorterThan(null)`/`LongerThan(null)` threw `NullReferenceException`; `InvalidFormat(null)` and `Empty(null)` threw `ArgumentNullException`, on the path whose whole contract is "returns an `Error`, never throws".
> 3. **`ToCamelCase("HTMLParser")` returned `"hTMLParser"`**; `"User ID".ToSnakeCase()` returned `"user id"`; `ToPascalCase` split words differently from `ToSnakeCase`.
> 4. **Guard messages depended on the server's culture.** `string.Format` used the current culture, so `OutOfRange` rendered `1,25` on a `tr-TR` host.
> 5. **`ToUnixMilliseconds` disagreed with the BCL** before 1970 (truncation toward zero vs `ToUnixTimeMilliseconds`'s floor: `0` vs `-1`), while duplicating it.
> 6. **`EndOfDay()` returned 23:59:59.999**, so a range query using it silently dropped rows in the day's last sub-millisecond.
> 7. **`new DomainException(null)` threw `NullReferenceException`** from `error.Message` in the base-constructor argument, not `ArgumentNullException`.
> 8. **`using SharedKernel.Guards;` did not make `Guard.Against.*` compile.** The extension methods lived in `SharedKernel.Guards.Clauses`, which no README example imported.
> 9. **No XML documentation shipped** — the same per-project `GenerateDocumentationFile` cause as P-529/C-127, confirmed by unzipping the nupkg (`.dll`, no `.xml`).
> 10. **Guard error codes were imprecise.** `ShorterThan`/`LongerThan`/`InvalidFormat`/`Email` all returned `validation.out_of_range` although `ErrorCodes.Validation.MinLength`/`MaxLength`/`InvalidFormat` exist.
> **BAD PRACTICES REMOVED:** `Guard.Throw.*` gave the compiler no null-state information (`[NotNull]`/`[DoesNotReturnIf]` added); every guard required `nameof(x)` (`[CallerArgumentExpression]`); `MaxCount`/`MinCount` enumerated the whole sequence (now `TryGetNonEnumeratedCount`, then stop at max+1 / min); the email regex used `RegexOptions.Compiled`, which NativeAOT ignores (`[GeneratedRegex]`); `InvalidFormat` put the regex pattern into a message that can reach an HTTP response; `NotPositive` was `NegativeOrZero` with a different message; the exception docs said "map `DomainException` to 422 / `NotFoundException` to 404" although `SharedKernelExceptionHandler` maps by `Error.Type`, so `new NotFoundException(Error.Validation(...))` returns 400; the package description, release notes and README carried internal WO/P IDs and a migration story for a `SharedKernel.Guards` package that never reached a feed.
> **ADDITIONS:** railway `Ensure`/`TapError`, the full railway for non-generic `Result`, `Bind` across `Result<T>`→`Result`, `GetValueOrThrow`/`ThrowIfFailure`; `ValueTask<Result…>` sources; `Task`-returning continuations on plain results; `ResultTry.Try(Action)`, `TryAsync(Func<Task>)` and `CancellationToken` overloads; `ForbiddenException` (403, distinct from `UnauthorizedException`'s 401) and `error.ToException()`; `Guard.Collect` and `Error?.ToResult(...)`; guards `LessThan`, `GreaterThan`, `InvalidEnumValue`, `NotUtc` (both date types), `Null` for `Nullable<T>`, generic-math `Negative<T>`/`NegativeOrZero<T>` over `INumber<T>` (NaN is a violation); `ToKebabCase`; `WhereNotNull` for `Nullable<T>`; `PublicApiAnalyzers` with `PublicAPI.Shipped.txt`/`PublicAPI.Unshipped.txt`.
> **ONE DESIGN CONSTRAINT FOUND BY THE COMPILER, record it, it generalizes:** a source may accept `Task`-returning *or* `ValueTask`-returning continuations, never both. With both present, an `async` lambda converts to either task-like type and the call is CS0121-ambiguous. Hence: `Task` sources and plain results take `Task` continuations, `ValueTask` sources take `ValueTask` continuations, and `ResultTry` has no `ValueTask` overloads. A lambda whose body only throws (`() => throw …`) has no return type and is ambiguous between the sync and async overloads; give it an explicit return type.
> **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE:** `Guard.Throw` does not throw `ArgumentException` (guards run in entity constructors, where a violation is an invariant breach and a 400 with a client-safe message is intended); exception constructors do not reject an `Error.Type` that disagrees with the subclass (throwing while constructing an exception hides the original failure); `SharedKernel.Validation`'s own guard extensions (`InvalidIban` and friends) were only re-pointed to the new namespace, not given `[CallerArgumentExpression]` — that is `SharedKernel.Validation`'s own pass; the repo-wide `Directory.Build.targets` `GenerateDocumentationFile` default was not fixed centrally (it would touch every package — CI scope); the SK0006 analyzer already on the feed still looks for `SharedKernel.Guards.Clauses.IGuardClause` and needs a republish before it polices consumers of this package.
> Depends on: P-529 (`SharedKernel.Primitives` must be on the feed before this package can publish).
> User-directed pre-publish hardening.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-102 | Audit the package by execution (probe app over every public member's edge cases) and by trim/AOT analyzer; rule with the user on which breaking changes to take while the package is unpublished | SharedKernel.Core | `●` |

| C-137 | Railway additions: `Ensure` (error or error factory), `TapError`, `Bind` to non-generic `Result`, `GetValueOrThrow`/`ThrowIfFailure`, the full railway on non-generic `Result`, `ValueTask` sources, `Task` continuations on plain results; `ResultTry.Try(Action)`, `TryAsync(Func<Task>)`, and four `CancellationToken` overloads that throw before invoking the delegate when already cancelled | SharedKernel.Core | `●` |

## Overall Progress

> Counts updated whenever a task state changes.
