All eleven are design-locked (every `D-*`/docs-only phase's design task marked `●` this pass where noted below); every `C-*`/`T-*`/`DO-*`/`P-*` task is `○` pending a future implementation pass. `P-523` (`00.Governance`, mechanically locking `SK.01.P518`'s convention) and `P-527`/`P-528` (`16.Testing`/`12.Security`, migrating their `ITotpReplayGuard` fakes for the already-shipped `SK.01.P514`) are each other domains' own phases, not tracked in this file. **`SK.01.P519` (5/5) has since shipped end to end** — `OptionsExtensions.AddValidatedOptions<TOptions, TValidator>(section)` added exactly per the locked D-93 design: no `.ValidateDataAnnotations()` call, `TValidator` registered via `services.TryAddSingleton<IValidateOptions<TOptions>, TValidator>()`, `.ValidateOnStart()` called identically to the existing overload; the pre-existing `AddValidatedOptions<TOptions>(section)` overload's own source is byte-for-byte untouched. A real, compiled `[OptionsValidator]`-annotated partial validator (`SourceGeneratedTestServiceOptionsValidator`, nested in a new `partial` test class since the generator emits a matching partial declaration on the containing type) proved the in-box BCL source generator genuinely compiles and validates against this new overload — not assumed from documentation; 6 new tests cover valid-config-starts-host, two invalid-config-throws-at-`IHost.StartAsync()` cases (missing `[Required]`, out-of-`[Range]`), two regression tests re-proving the DataAnnotations overload's own behavior is unchanged, and a `TryAddSingleton`-idempotency proof (calling the new overload twice registers the validator exactly once). 10/10 `SharedKernel.Configuration.Tests` passing (up from 4/4). `01.Core/README.md` and the package's own `README.md` both gained a "Source-Generated Options Validation (opt-in, AOT-clean)" section showing the `[OptionsValidator]` pattern beside the existing default. Repacked to the local feed at `1.0.0-alpha.0.867` (no `<Version>`/git tag — `devops-lead`'s call per established precedent). **`SK.01.P520` (3/3) has since shipped end to end** — `SharedKernel.Primitives.csproj`'s `PackageReleaseNotes` rewritten to present-tense/lockstep-accurate prose (no per-package version number); `SharedKernel.Cryptography.csproj`'s `<Description>` had its stray `"v2.0.0:"` prefix reworded to cite the shipped phase/work-order instead; this file's own Package Board had its five stale `SharedKernel.Primitives`/`SharedKernel.Core`/`SharedKernel.Configuration`/`SharedKernel.FeatureManagement`/`SharedKernel.Guards` rows corrected to Current Phase "Published"/State `●`, and gained a new `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2` row; both affected packages repacked to the local feed (no `<Version>`/git tag — devops-lead's call, per the established precedent). Docs-only, zero `.cs` diff, as designed. **`SK.01.P516` (5/5) has since shipped end to end** — `Seal()`/`IsSealed` added to `InMemoryLocalizationCatalog` exactly per the locked D-90 design (an `Interlocked`-backed sealed flag, `AddTranslation` throwing `InvalidOperationException` once sealed, zero lock overhead on `TryGetString`'s read path), `AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog`'s DI factory calling `catalog.Seal()` immediately after its `configure` callback returns so the shipped DI path is safe with zero consumer action; full XML docs on the seed-once-then-immutable lifecycle including the explicit note that `StringLocalizerLocalizationCatalog` is unaffected; new concurrency-focused tests (a 16-thread/2,000-iteration-per-thread concurrent `TryGetString` proof against a sealed catalog, a post-seal `AddTranslation` non-corruption proof, and a DI-resolution-is-already-sealed proof) — 52/52 `SharedKernel.Localization.Tests` passing (up from 43/43); repacked to the local feed (`SharedKernel.Localization.1.0.0-alpha.0.867.nupkg`, no `<Version>`/git tag, `devops-lead`'s call per established precedent). **`SK.01.P524` (6/6) has since shipped end to end** — `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher.Hash`/`.Verify`, `HmacSha256Signer.Verify`, and `AesGcmEncryptionService`'s four String-based overloads (`EncryptToString(Async)`/`DecryptToString(Async)`) now zero every buffer this package genuinely owns and controls the lifetime of via `CryptographicOperations.ZeroMemory` inside `try`/`finally`, exactly per the ratified D-97 target set — the primary `byte[]`-based `Encrypt`/`Decrypt`(`Async`) overloads' directly-returned buffers are untouched, as designed; `Argon2idOneWayHasher.Hash`/`.Verify` (and their shared `ComputeSubkey` helper's intermediate `secretBytes`) got the identical treatment in `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2`. T-92 shipped as internal, `[InternalsVisibleTo]`-gated capture-before-zeroing overloads (e.g. `internal string Hash(string secret, Action<byte[]>? captureSubkeyForTesting)`) on each target method — never a static mutable hook, keeping this domain's "no static mutable state" rule intact — each with a same-named public wrapper passing `null`, so the public surface is unchanged; four new tests (`Hash_ZeroesTheDerivedSubkeyBuffer_BeforeReturning` in both `Pbkdf2OneWayHasherTests`/`Argon2idOneWayHasherTests`, `Verify_ZeroesTheInternalExpectedComparisonBuffer_BeforeReturning` in `HmacSha256SignerTests`, `EncryptToString_ZeroesTheIntermediateUtf8PlaintextBuffer_BeforeReturning` in `AesGcmEncryptionServiceTests`, plus a companion `Decrypt_PrimaryByteArrayOverload_NeverZeroesItsOwnReturnValue` proof) capture the exact same array reference the production code zeroes and assert every byte is genuinely `0` — not merely that `ZeroMemory` is referenced in source. `SharedKernel.Cryptography/README.md` gained a full "Key-Material Zeroization" section (which buffers are zeroed and when, the two declined broader asks — no `IDisposable CryptographicKey`, no `ReadOnlySpan<char>`/`char[]` `IOneWayHasher` overloads — restated with their rationale, and upstream secret-lifetime-minimization guidance for a caller before a secret ever reaches this package). 322/322 `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Tests` passing (up from 318/318), 46/46 `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2.Tests` passing (up from 45/45); both packages repacked to the local feed at `1.0.0-alpha.0.867` (no `<Version>`/git tag, `devops-lead`'s call per established precedent). **`SK.01.P521` (5/5) has since shipped end to end** — `IbanValidator`/`IsoCurrencyValidator`/`IsoCountryValidator` each gained a public `RegistryAsOf` constant (a last-reviewed marker, honestly framed as NOT a formal ISO/SWIFT registry version number since none of ISO 13616/4217/3166 publish one in that shape); `IbanValidator.Validate`/`.IsValid` gained the opt-in trailing `allowFallbackForUnknownCountry = false` parameter exactly per the locked D-95 design — default unchanged (an unrecognized country prefix still hard-rejects with `ValidationErrorCodes.Iban.InvalidFormat`), and when explicitly `true` an unrecognized prefix instead skips only the country-specific exact-length check, still enforcing ISO 13616's general 34-character bound, the alphanumeric BBAN shape, and the mod-97 check-digit algorithm in full. New tests (`Mod97ValidUnknownCountryIban`/`Mod97InvalidUnknownCountryIban` under the unrecognized prefix `"ZZ"`, both independently derived via the real ISO 13616 check-digit computation, not invented) prove: the default-mode regression (a mod-97-valid value under an unrecognized country still hard-rejects unless explicitly opted in); fallback-mode acceptance of the mod-97-valid case; fallback-mode still rejecting the mod-97-invalid case (proving the fallback trades away length checking, never checksum correctness); a general-bound-exceeding (35-character) unrecognized-country value still rejected as `InvalidLength` even in fallback mode; and all three `RegistryAsOf` constants are non-empty. 180/180 `SharedKernel.Validation.Tests` passing (up from 172/172). `SharedKernel.Validation/README.md` gained a "Table Refresh Cadence" section documenting the `RegistryAsOf` constants and the fallback parameter's usage/trade-off. The `.FluentValidation` adapter's matching `.MustBeValidIban(allowFallbackForUnknownCountry:)` overload remains explicitly out of scope for this phase, as designed. Repacked to the local feed (no `<Version>`/git tag, `devops-lead`'s call per established precedent). **`SK.01.P518` (14/14) has since shipped end to end** — all eight DI extension methods converted exactly per the locked D-92 design (`SharedKernel.Cryptography`'s `AddSharedKernelCryptography`, `SharedKernel.Compression`'s `AddSharedKernelCompression`, `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2`'s `AddSharedKernelArgon2Cryptography`, `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography`, `SharedKernel.FeatureManagement`'s `AddSharedKernelFeatureManagement`, `SharedKernel.Localization`'s two catalog registrations, `SharedKernel.Primitives`' `ClockExtensions.AddClock()`, `SharedKernel.Validation`'s `AddNationalIdValidator<TValidator>()` via `TryAddEnumerable` exactly as D-92 specified); T-85 (double-call idempotency across all eleven `SharedKernel.Cryptography` services plus `SharedKernel.Compression`'s three), T-86 (pre-registered consumer fake wins, across `SharedKernel.Cryptography`/`SharedKernel.Compression`/`SharedKernel.FeatureManagement`/`SharedKernel.Primitives`), T-87 (the `AddNationalIdValidator<TValidator>()` regression pair — two distinct country validators both survive; the same validator registered twice collapses to one), and T-88 (the `SharedKernel.Localization` first-wins inversion, both call orders) all shipped as new tests; DO-48 shipped as XML-doc updates on every converted method plus a new "DI Registration Conventions" section in `01.Core/README.md`; P-51 shipped as a local-feed repack of all eight touched packages (plus `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation`, repacked for its own unrelated fix — see below) at `1.0.0-alpha.0.867`. **A second genuine regression, of the identical class D-92 already caught for `INationalIdValidator`, was found and fixed during this implementation pass, not anticipated by the phase's own design:** `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptionsValidator` was converted to a plain `TryAddSingleton<IValidateOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions>, ...>()` per C-110's literal instruction — but `IValidateOptions<TOptions>` is ALSO a multi-implementation collection type (the options-validation pipeline runs every registered validator for a type, not just one), and the immediately preceding `AddValidatedOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions>(section)` call already registers the BCL's own `DataAnnotationValidateOptions<T>` against that identical service type via `ValidateDataAnnotations()`. The plain `TryAddSingleton` therefore silently never registered the custom cross-field validator — confirmed by two host-startup tests (`EmptyKeyNames_ThrowsAtHostStartup`, `CurrentKeyIdNotInKeyNames_ThrowsAtHostStartup`) that stopped throwing. Fixed to `TryAddEnumerable(ServiceDescriptor.Singleton<IValidateOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions>, AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptionsValidator>())`, with matching XML-doc corrections on both the extension method and the validator itself, plus a new regression test (`CalledTwice_CrossFieldValidatorStillEnforced`) proving the fix survives repeated calls to `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography()` — 70/70 `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure.Tests` passing (up from 69/69, two of which were silently broken before this fix and are now green again). Also fixed as a separate, pre-existing build break found before this phase started: `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation`'s `MustBeValidIban<T>()` failed to compile against P-521's new optional `IbanValidator.Validate(string?, bool)` parameter (an optional parameter still changes method-group-conversion arity) — resolved by exposing the matching `allowFallbackForUnknownCountry = false` parameter on the adapter rule itself (the small follow-on P-521's own notes flagged as out of scope, now in scope since the build needed it), with 3 new tests (39/39 `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation.Tests`, up from 36/36) proving the default preserves prior behavior and the fallback genuinely forwards. Motivation: `05.Application`'s `FailureResponseFactory`/`ResultOfTDispatcher<TResponse>` (P-232, WO-038) claimed to have eliminated reflection via `IResultOfT<T>` but still calls `Type.GetInterfaces()`/`MakeGenericType()`/`GetMethod()`/`Invoke()` — invisible to governance's SK0012 rule but exactly the shape it exists to eliminate; this phase gives `05.Application` (P-237) the primitive needed to genuinely close the gap. Additive only — `IHasSuccessFlag`, `IResultOfT<T>`, and every existing `Result<T>`/`Result` member signature unchanged. Total tasks now 146 (core-arch-planner, WO-039)

Added 8 tests to `ResultInterfaceTests.cs` (dispatch for `Result<int>`/`Result<string>`, assignability, exclusion proof). While wiring these in, found and fixed a pre-existing broken test (`IResultOfT_GenericConstraintPattern_WorksWithoutReflection`, committed in 5e9ed3a) that used an unsatisfiable self-referential constraint (`where TResponse : IResultOfT<TResponse>`) against `Result<T> : IResultOfT<T>`, which was blocking `SharedKernel.Primitives.Tests` from compiling at all; corrected to the two-type-parameter form (`where TResponse : IResultOfT<TValue>`) matching `IResultOfT<T>`'s actual (non-self-referential) shape. 74/74 `SharedKernel.Primitives.Tests` passing; `SK.01.P236` now fully `●` (5/5); propagated to root `state-map.md` (core-phase-implementer)

- [2026-07-27] S-19/C-51/C-52/T-41/DO-22/P-13/P-14/P-15 → ● in SK.01.P297 — shipped the seventh `01.Core` package, `SharedKernel.Compression`, exactly per the D-38/D-39/D-40 locked design: `IPayloadCompressor` (byte[]/stream/async overloads), `BrotliPayloadCompressor` (unkeyed default + "Brotli"-keyed singleton) and `GZipPayloadCompressor` ("GZip"-keyed singleton only), `CompressionOptions` (`.Level`, `[EnumDataType]`-validated), `AddSharedKernelCompression`. Empirically discovered and corrected a design-doc inaccuracy during implementation: `Decompress` must catch `InvalidOperationException` in addition to `InvalidDataException` — `BrotliStream`'s decoder throws the former ("Decoder ran into invalid data"), not `InvalidDataException`, for corrupt input. Also empirically discovered (verified against this BCL version, not assumed) that neither `BrotliStream` nor `GZipStream` reliably detects a _suffix_-truncated compressed stream as an error — `GZipStream` does not validate its trailing CRC32/ISIZE footer on read, and `BrotliStream` has no fixed magic-number header the way gzip does, so truncation detection is asserted per-algorithm in tests (gzip: prefix-truncation reliably fails via its magic number; Brotli: only the "never throws unhandled" guarantee is asserted) rather than as a single shared contract test — documented in both XML docs and `01.Core/README.md`'s new "A note on truncation detection" subsection, recommending `IContentHasher`/a known length for services that must guarantee end-to-end truncation detection. 46/46 `SharedKernel.Compression.Tests` passing, 0 build warnings under `GenerateDocumentationFile`; packed to `./nupkgs` as `SharedKernel.Compression.1.0.0`; `SharedKernel.Consumer.Tests` extended with Compression DI/roundtrip/corruption checks, 46/46 passing (Primitives + Configuration transitive chain confirmed). `SK.01.P297` now fully `●` (11/11); propagated to root `state-map.md` (core-phase-implementer)

Empirically discovered and fixed a real pre-existing defect, not assumed from prose: `AddSharedKernelFeatureManagement` passed `configuration.GetSection("FeatureManagement")` into `Microsoft.FeatureManagement`'s own `AddFeatureManagement`, silently making the variant/allocation schema (`feature_management:feature_flags`, an unscoped, differently-named root key) completely unreachable — confirmed via a throwaway console harness against the real `4.5.0` package before writing adapter code; fixed by passing the root `IConfiguration` instead, which matches this method's pre-existing public signature (no consumer-visible change) and leaves plain boolean `FeatureManagement:*` flags resolving identically. `GetVariantAsync<TContext>` bridges Microsoft's variant API, which (confirmed via reflection) is fixed to a concrete `ITargetingContext` (UserId+Groups) with no generic per-`TContext` equivalent to `IsEnabledAsync<TContext>`, by deriving the targeting identity from `context?.ToString()` — documented explicitly, deterministic by construction. The two pre-existing `IsEnabledAsync` members keep discarding `ct` exactly as before (the newly-injected interface technically accepts it now, but forwarding it was deliberately declined to guarantee byte-for-byte-unchanged behavior per this phase's hard rule). Added `FeatureVariantTests.cs` (29 total `SharedKernel.FeatureManagement.Tests`: 9 pre-existing + 20 new, including a reflection-based test asserting `IFeatureManager`'s public surface never exposes a `Microsoft.FeatureManagement` type). `01.Core/README.md` gained a "Feature Variants — Gradual Rollout" section (dual-schema configuration requirement, percentage-based tenant example, determinism caveat for non-string contexts, and the flagged non-blocking pre-existing AOT-manifest gap, not worsened by this phase). 29/29 `SharedKernel.FeatureManagement.Tests` passing, 0 build warnings; `SK.01.P298` now fully `●` (5/5) — every WO-049 phase inside `01.Core`'s own jurisdiction (P-292→P-298) is complete; propagated to root `state-map.md` (core-phase-implementer)

- [2026-08-26] P-446 processed (WO-068) — locked design for a **breaking** change to `IEncryptionKeyProvider`: `GetCurrentKey()`/`GetKey(string)` become `GetCurrentKeyAsync`/`GetKeyAsync` (`CancellationToken`-aware, `ValueTask`-returning), the sync members removed outright (not retained as an overload). `ISymmetricEncryptionService` gains additive `EncryptAsync`/`DecryptAsync`/`EncryptToStringAsync`/`DecryptToStringAsync`; the existing sync `Encrypt`/`Decrypt` are retained and internally bridge to the async provider via `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()` — genuinely non-blocking for the config-based default and a cache hit, a real thread-block only for a genuinely network-bound provider on a cache miss, documented IN CAPITALS on every retained sync member. This deliberate design choice is what keeps `06.Persistence`'s structurally-synchronous-only EF Core `ValueConverter` pipeline solvable in its own follow-on phase (P-448, out of this domain's jurisdiction) rather than forcing a platform-wide breaking cascade across `02.Caching`/`06.Persistence`/`07.Messaging`/`15.Integration`/`17.Workflows` from this one phase. Fail-closed is structural — an unreachable KMS always propagates as a thrown exception, never a silent no-encryption fallback. Because `IStringLocalizer`'s indexer carries no per-call culture parameter in this framework version (confirmed by reflecting over the installed `Microsoft.Extensions.Localization.Abstractions` 10.0.11 assembly — no `WithCulture` member exists), `TryGetString` temporarily swaps the ambient `CultureInfo.CurrentUICulture` for the duration of the synchronous call and restores it in a `finally` block, including on exception — covered by tests. `LocalizationServiceCollectionExtensions.AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog(...)`/`.AddStringLocalizerCatalog<TResource>()` implemented; T-61's naming-collision guard is a real reflection-based scan over every public static method this assembly declares, asserting none is named `AddSharedKernelLocalization` (reserved for `13.ServiceDefaults`'s P-483), with a companion non-vacuous-check test proving the scan actually finds the two sanctioned method names. 43/43 `SharedKernel.Localization.Tests` passing, including a `ReadmeSampleCompileTests.cs` compiling every README code sample verbatim (the composition-root `AddLocalization()` sample needed a test-only `Microsoft.Extensions.Localization`+`Microsoft.Extensions.Logging` package reference to resolve `IStringLocalizerFactory`/`ILoggerFactory` at runtime — neither is a dependency of the shipped package itself, which references only `.Abstractions`). All five design-locked (`D-84`/`D-85`/`D-86`/`D-87`/`D-88` all `●`); implementation (`C-*`/`T-*`/`DO-*`/`P-*`) left `○`. All five mutually independent, meant to implement as one batch. **Two of the five corrected a factually wrong premise in their own dispatched brief, found by reading real, current source rather than trusting the brief's prose** — consistent with the bar this same audit already set across WO-081's twenty-one prior corrections. **P-510** (SEVERE) — `ResultTry`'s default exception mapping stops leaking a caught exception's raw `"{Type}: {Message}"` into `Error.Message`; the exception detail instead flows through `System.Diagnostics.Activity.Current?.AddException(...)`, a real .NET 8+ BCL member (zero new dependency) that records it on the ambient OTel trace span — honoring, not working around, this same audit's ratified `⊘ DECLINED` "no metadata bag on `Error`" ruling (root `state-map.md`, 2026-09-04) by using a channel the platform's own Logging Conventions already treat as ambient (mirrors CorrelationId/TraceId propagation). Also narrows every `ResultTry` catch clause to exclude `OperationCanceledException`, which must always propagate uncaught. **P-511** — fixes a cancellation-token leak in every `Lazy<Task<T>>`-shaped single-flight cache this domain ships, via a dedicated per-slot `CancellationTokenSource` (never derived from a caller's token) plus `Task.WaitAsync(callerCt)` so each caller observes only its own cancellation. **SCOPE CORRECTED during design**: the brief's named "`AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s current-key process-lifetime slot" no longer exists — P-496 (shipped, same WO-081 batch) replaced it with a durable version registry — so the fix instead targets P-496's own two new caches (`_resolvedKeysByCacheKey`, `_plaintextKeysByTag`), plus the pre-existing `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` cache the brief also named, PLUS a fourth site not named in the brief at all, found by auditing the same package family: `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider`'s (P-494) identical `_resolvedKeysByAzureKeyName` cache. **P-512** — a real PBKDF2 iteration floor (`MinimumPbkdf2Iterations = 100_000`, enforced via the already-wired `[Range]`/`ValidateOnStart` path) closing the "`Pbkdf2Iterations: 1` passes startup validation cleanly" gap, plus a `Verify`-time ceiling (`MaxVerifiableIterations = 2_000_000`, a fixed constant deliberately independent of the configured value, checked BEFORE the expensive `Rfc2898DeriveBytes.Pbkdf2` call) and an exact-32-byte stored-subkey-length check closing a second, independent CPU-exhaustion vector found while designing the first — a stored hash's iteration count AND subkey length are both attacker-influenceable, and the self-describing-format design's own "raising the configured count never invalidates existing hashes" guarantee is preserved by never enforcing the new floor retroactively at `Verify` time. **P-513** — a structural 32-byte `CryptographicKey.Material` length check inside `AesGcmEncryptionService.EnsureKeySize`, called before any `AesGcm` construction in both `EncryptCore`/`DecryptCore`, throwing `CryptographicException` rather than silently constructing AES-128/AES-192-GCM from a misconfigured shorter/longer key. **P-514** (BREAKING) — `ITotpReplayGuard`'s two-step `HasBeenUsedAsync`+`MarkUsedAsync` TOCTOU is collapsed into one atomic `TryMarkUsedAsync` (mirrors `18.Idempotency`'s atomic-reservation shape); `TotpVerifier` gains optional `digits`/`stepSeconds`/`driftWindow`/`algorithm` parameters (defaulted to the exact values the two now-removed hardcoded constants used) so its replay window is derived from what was actually validated; a new standalone `ITotpAttemptThrottle` seam is added, deliberately never wired into - [2026-09-08] Six phases processed in a single pass for WO-081 — a coordinated, `01.Core`-first breaking wave whose contracts six other domains' own planners (`02.Caching` P-497, `06.Persistence` P-498, `07.Messaging` P-499, `15.Integration` P-500, `17.Workflows` P-501, `16.Testing` P-502) plus `13.ServiceDefaults` (P-503) and `00.Governance` (P-504) dispatch against once this file's design is read. All six design-locked (every `D-*` task `●`); `S-*`/`C-*`/`T-*`/`DO-*`/`P-*` implementation tasks left `○`, mirroring the WO-067/068/069/076/078 precedent. **P-491** (`SK.01.P491`, breaking) — every `ISymmetricEncryptionService` member gains a required `byte[] associatedData` parameter, no defaulted overload; `AesGcmEncryptionService` passes it through to `AesGcm`'s own `associatedData` parameter; `EncryptedPayload` gains no new field, AAD is never persisted; a full six-domain production call-site inventory (`02.Caching`'s `CacheEncryptionSerializer`, `06.Persistence`'s `EncryptedValueConverter<T>` — flagged as the hardest, no direct row-PK access inside a vanilla EF Core `ValueConverter`, left to `06.Persistence`'s own P-498 — `07.Messaging`'s payload-transform trio, `15.Integration`'s webhook encryption, `17.Workflows`'s `EncryptionPayloadCodec`, `16.Testing`'s `AddFakeCryptography` fake) recorded directly in the phase's own notes per its explicit acceptance criterion. **P-492** (`SK.01.P492`) — `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` capability marker + `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous`, replacing the silent `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()` thread-blocking hazard on the four retained sync `ISymmetricEncryptionService` members with a structural `NotSupportedException`; `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` gains a public `.Inner` property so the capability check recursively unwraps to the true leaf provider rather than trusting the decorator's own best-case assumption — `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` itself never directly implements the marker. **P-493** (`SK.01.P493`, breaking, depends on P-492) — `IAsymmetricKeyProvider.GetRsaKey`/`GetEcdsaKey` become `GetRsaKeyAsync`/`GetEcdsaKeyAsync` (sync members removed outright); `IAsymmetricSignatureService` gains `SignAsync`/`VerifyAsync`, retained sync `Sign`/`Verify` gated by a new analogous `ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider`/`AsymmetricKeyProviderCapabilities` pair; `RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService` stop disposing the provider-returned key instance (fixes a latent `ObjectDisposedException` hazard invisible until P-494 makes it reachable) and `Verify` gains the same `EnsureMinimumKeySize` check `Sign` already had. Recorded explicitly, not glossed over: `RSA`/`ECDsa`'s BCL surface has no async `SignData`/`VerifyData` overload at all, so even `SignAsync`/`VerifyAsync` only make **key resolution** non-blocking — the actual cryptographic call against a remote-KMS-backed key remains a genuine, unavoidable synchronous network operation, a limitation P-494 carries forward at the exact point it becomes concretely observable. **P-494** (`SK.01.P494`, depends on P-493) — a new `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` (deliberately separate from `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`) backing `GetRsaKeyAsync`/`GetEcdsaKeyAsync` via `CryptographyClient.SignData`/`VerifyData` through thin `KeyVaultRsaKey : RSA`/`KeyVaultEcdsaKey : ECDsa` subclasses (`ExportParameters`/`ImportParameters` throw `NotSupportedException` — no private key material ever crosses the process boundary); bakes in a per-key-name `CryptographyClient` cache from its very first implementation specifically so P-496 does not have to fix the same connection-pooling defect twice. **P-495** (`SK.01.P495`, independent of every other WO-081 phase) — a new, **thirteenth `01.Core` package**, `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2` (`Konscious.Security.Cryptography.Argon2`, pure-managed, no native binding): `Argon2idOneWayHasher` emitting the real, interoperable PHC string format (`$argon2id$v=19$m=...,t=...,p=...$salt$hash`) rather than a bespoke encoding, registered as a `"Argon2id"`-**keyed-only** singleton via `AddSharedKernelArgon2Cryptography` — `Pbkdf2OneWayHasher` stays the sole unkeyed default and the FIPS-mode choice, completely untouched; `Argon2CryptographyOptions` defaults (`MemorySizeKb=19456`, `Iterations=2`, `DegreeOfParallelism=1`) cite OWASP's current Argon2id minimum recommendation explicitly. **P-496** (`SK.01.P496`, depends on P-494) — three related `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` redesigns: (1) the same per-key-name `CryptographyClient` reuse P-494 already established; (2) `CryptographicKey.Id` becomes a short, durable version tag (`"v1"`/`"v2"`) backed by a new `Azure.Security.KeyVault.Secrets`-persisted version registry plus a shared `"current version"` pointer secret every replica reads — correcting a real, previously-undocumented P-447 gap where every process/pod silently minted its own unique "current" data key with no genuine rotation intent or cross-replica sharing; `GetKeyAsync` stays backward-read-compatible via a dual-shape fallback (new short-tag lookup first, the legacy self-decodable envelope parse second), so this ships as an **additive MINOR repack, not a breaking one** — a deliberate choice recorded explicitly, rejecting the alternative of a forced-data-migration breaking release; (3) a new explicitly-callable (never automatic) `MintNewVersionAsync`, giving the provider its first real rotation story, with every previously-minted version staying resolvable indefinitely. 58 tasks added across six new phase keys (`SK.01.P491`→`SK.01.P496`; D-65→D-83, S-25, C-81→C-95, T-66→T-74, DO-34→DO-39, P-35→P-42); total tasks now 365 (core-arch-planner, WO-081)

D-76's table row above has been rewritten in place to state the corrected `SignHash`/`VerifyHash` shape as authoritative, rather than leaving the contradiction for the next reader to trip over. Implementation: `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` (sealed, `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` only, distinct singleton from `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`, reuses `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions.KeyNames`/`.VaultUri`/`.Credential`) resolves each distinct Azure key name's `KeyVaultKey` metadata (`KeyType`, and a `KeySize` derived via `JsonWebKey.ToRSA(false)`/`.ToECDsa(false)` — public material only, immediately disposed after reading `.KeySize`, never a hand-rolled modulus-byte-length computation) plus one `CryptographyClient` exactly once per Azure key name, cached in a `ConcurrentDictionary<string, Lazy<Task<ResolvedAzureKey>>>` (mirrors `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s own `Lazy<Task<CryptographicKey>>` "current key" slot pattern, including clearing a failed entry so the next call retries) — never constructed per call, baking in from day one the exact connection-reuse fix P-496 has to apply retroactively to the sibling class. An unrecognized `keyId` throws `KeyNotFoundException` purely locally (`ResolveAzureKeyName`, a dictionary lookup — no Azure call attempted), per `IAsymmetricKeyProvider`'s documented contract; a `keyId` resolving to the wrong Azure key type (RSA requested against an EC key or vice versa) throws `InvalidOperationException`. `KeyVaultRsaKey`/`KeyVaultEcdsaKey` (internal sealed, package-private) override the REAL BCL extension points — `SignHash(byte[] hash, HashAlgorithmName, RSASignaturePadding)`/`VerifyHash(...)` for RSA, `SignHash(byte[] hash)`/`VerifyHash(byte[] hash, byte[] signature)` for ECDSA (no algorithm parameter at all on the ECDSA overrides, so the 32-byte SHA-256 digest length is the discriminant instead) — delegating to `CryptographyClient`'s real synchronous, HASH-taking `Sign(SignatureAlgorithm, byte[] digest, ct)`/`Verify(SignatureAlgorithm, byte[] digest, byte[] signature, ct)` overloads (confirmed via direct reflection against the installed `Azure.Security.KeyVault.Keys` 4.7.0 assembly in a throwaway scratch console probe, not assumed from prose — `CryptographyClient.SignData`/`VerifyData` exist too but take raw data, not a hash, and are the wrong overload entirely), mapping the one fixed combination each signature service actually requests (SHA-256+PSS → `PS256`; SHA-256 on P-256 → `ES256`) and throwing `NotSupportedException` for anything else. A genuine, unrelated BCL naming collision was hit and fixed: `AsymmetricAlgorithm.SignatureAlgorithm` (an inherited instance `string` property) shadows the `Azure...SignatureAlgorithm` TYPE name for unqualified resolution inside a class deriving from `RSA`/`ECDsa`, producing `CS0120`/`CS1061` — resolved via a `using AzureSignatureAlgorithm = Azure.Security.KeyVault.Keys.Cryptography.SignatureAlgorithm;` alias in both files, confirmed by a real compile failure then a real fix, not guessed. `ExportParameters`/`ImportParameters` throw `NotSupportedException` on both (`GenerateKey` too, for ECDSA) — private key material never crosses the process boundary; `KeySize`'s setter also throws (the vault key's size cannot be changed locally). `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` never implements `ISynchronousAsymmetricKeyProvider` — proven directly via `AsymmetricKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous` returning `false` against it, and via `RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService`'s sync `Sign` throwing `NotSupportedException` when constructed against it. `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` extended to additionally register `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProvider` as `IAsymmetricKeyProvider` — a distinct singleton, proven via `Assert.NotSame` against the encryption provider's own service-type registrations. T-71: `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProviderTests.cs` (12 locally-decidable unit tests — unknown-`keyId` `KeyNotFoundException` for both algorithms without any Azure call, null-argument guards, `RsaSignatureService`/`EcdsaSignatureService` constructing against the provider with zero code changes to either class, sync `Sign` throwing `NotSupportedException`) plus `AzureKeyVaultAsymmetricKeyProviderIntegrationTests.cs` (mirrors T-54's/T-65's/P-493's own real-`CryptographyClient`-attempting-a-real-loopback-connection technique — genuinely executed every run: unreachable-vault fail-closed for both `GetRsaKeyAsync`/`GetEcdsaKeyAsync`; env-gated via new, DELIBERATELY DISTINCT `SHAREDKERNEL_TEST_AZURE_KEYVAULT_RSA_SIGNING_KEY_NAME`/`_ECDSA_SIGNING_KEY_NAME` variables — never reusing the encryption provider's own `SHAREDKERNEL_TEST_AZURE_KEYVAULT_KEY_NAME`, since a wrap/unwrap data-encryption key and a sign/verify key are configured with different Key Vault key operations and are not interchangeable — honestly reports a skip via `ITestOutputHelper` and asserts nothing when unset, exactly as it is in this sandbox: not exercised here). 53/53 `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure.Tests` green, 0 warnings, 0 errors. - [2026-09-08] C-92→C-95, T-73, T-74, DO-39 → ● in SK.01.P496 (P-42 → `⚑`) — implemented `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`'s connection-reuse/durable-version-registry/rotation hardening exactly per the locked D-80→D-83 design, with two implementation-time refinements over the design's own literal wording (found and corrected while implementing, not merely followed — the same discipline P-494's D-76 correction established, and explicitly flagged as expected in this pass's own dispatch brief): (1) the connection-reuse cache is keyed by (Azure key name, key VERSION) rather than Azure key name alone, since `UnwrapDataKeyAsync` must be able to pin to the EXACT historical Azure key version that wrapped a given data key (which can differ from "current" after an out-of-band Azure-side key rotation between two local mints) — keying by name alone would silently collide two real key versions onto one cache slot; (2) `UnwrapDataKeyAsync`'s `masterKeyId` parser defensively accepts both the new short `"{azureKeyName}/{version}"` shape AND the legacy full Key Vault key identifier URI shape, not new-shape-only, since the envelope-wrap path's caller (never this provider) is the durable store for that value. New `VersionSecretPayload`/`AzureKeyVaultJsonSerializerContext` (source-generated STJ, no reflection) and a new `SecretClient` field back the durable registry — each minted tag is its own Key Vault Secret; a shared `"current version"` pointer secret is read LIVE on every `GetCurrentKeyAsync` call (no internal caching of which tag is current, by design — bounded-TTL caching of that stays `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`'s job, composed externally, unchanged). `GetCurrentKeyAsync` throws `InvalidOperationException` when nothing has ever been minted rather than auto-minting — auto-minting would silently reintroduce the exact per-process "current key" accident this whole phase exists to eliminate. `MintNewVersionAsync(CancellationToken) → ValueTask<string>` mints, wraps, stores, and atomically repoints the pointer; XML docs state in capitals it provides no cross-caller mutual exclusion (an accepted, documented limitation — no data is ever lost by a race, only the pointer). T-73 required a call-counting test double around `CryptographyClient`/`SecretClient` rather than a live-network assertion — genuinely new engineering for this domain: a new `TestSupport/AzureKeyVaultCallCountingFakes.cs` SUBCLASSES the real, non-sealed `KeyClient`/`SecretClient`/`CryptographyClient` types directly (their `protected` parameterless constructors and `virtual` members make this legal from outside the SDK's own assembly — confirmed via a throwaway reflection probe before writing any fake code, not assumed), constructed through a NEW `internal`-only test-seam constructor overload on `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` exposed via a project-scoped `InternalsVisibleTo` — deliberately narrow, since every extra parameter is unregistered in DI with no default value, so MS.DI's automatic constructor-selection can never select it for the real `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` registration. Azure SDK model types (`KeyVaultKey`/`WrapResult`/`UnwrapResult`) expose only an `internal` constructor and/or `internal`-setter properties from outside the SDK's own assembly, so constructing realistic fake return values required reflection over those exact members — isolated entirely inside that one test-support file, never production code, and confirmed to actually work via the same scratch-probe discipline before committing to the approach. `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProviderVersionRegistryTests.cs` (9 tests) covers T-73's four required criteria (a superseded version still decrypts after a mint; a second `GetKeyAsync` for an already-resolved tag costs zero further Key Vault calls; two independently-constructed provider instances sharing one simulated vault converge `GetCurrentKeyAsync` on the identical tag; a legacy pre-P-496 envelope-shaped `keyId` still resolves via the fallback path) plus three bonus assertions (`GetCurrentKeyAsync` throws before any mint; an unknown-but-tag-shaped `keyId` returns `null` via 404 translation; repeated mints reuse one cached Azure-key resolution — C-92's own claim, proven directly via a call count). T-74 added `MintNewVersionAsync_UnreachableVault_ThrowsInsteadOfSilentNoOp` (always exercised, mirrors every sibling member's fail-closed proof) and an env-gated `MintNewVersionAsync_ThenEncryptUnderNewCurrent_ThenDecryptUnderOldTag_RoundTripsAgainstRealAzureKeyVault` (mirrors T-54's/T-65's/T-71's existing skip-if-unavailable-gated real-vault posture exactly, no new harness — including a length assertion confirming a newly-minted `CryptographicKey.Id` is short). DO-39: XML-doc'd every changed/new member (including the two design refinements above, recorded explicitly rather than silently); `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure/README.md` gained a "Key rotation and the durable version registry" section with the `MintNewVersionAsync` recipe; `01.Core/README.md`'s Azure Key Vault section gained matching "Connection reuse"/"durable version registry"/"Key rotation" content plus package-table and dependency-diagram updates; `01.Core/CLAUDE.md`'s Packages/Interface Contracts/Test Rules sections updated from "design-locked, implementation pending" to "SHIPPED". **BLOCKED, honestly reported rather than worked around, exactly per this phase's own explicit dispatch instruction**: this phase's `.csproj` adds a `PackageReference` to `Azure.Security.KeyVault.Secrets` with no corresponding root-owned `Directory.Packages.props` `PackageVersion` entry — `dotnet build`/`dotnet pack`/`dotnet test` on this package (and anything transitively referencing it) fail with NU1010 in this repository as committed. - [2026-09-09] C-107–C-114, T-85–T-88, DO-48, P-51 → ● in SK.01.P518, all tasks complete (14/14) — implemented the domain-wide `TryAdd`/`TryAddEnumerable` DI-registration standardization exactly per the locked D-92 design: all eight DI extension methods converted from plain `AddSingleton`/`AddKeyedSingleton` to `TryAddSingleton`/`TryAddKeyedSingleton` (`SharedKernel.Cryptography`'s `AddSharedKernelCryptography` — 11 services; `SharedKernel.Compression`'s `AddSharedKernelCompression` — 3; `SharedKernel.Cryptography.Argon2`'s `AddSharedKernelArgon2Cryptography` — 1; `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s `AddSharedKernelAzureKeyVaultCryptography` — 7 plain + the one exception below; `SharedKernel.FeatureManagement`'s own `IFeatureManager` line; `SharedKernel.Localization`'s two catalog factories; `SharedKernel.Primitives`' `ClockExtensions.AddClock()`), with `SharedKernel.Validation`'s `AddNationalIdValidator<TValidator>()` correctly converted to `TryAddEnumerable(ServiceDescriptor.Singleton<INationalIdValidator, TValidator>())` rather than a plain `TryAddSingleton`, preserving the multi-country collection semantics `INationalIdValidatorRegistry` depends on. T-85 (`AddSharedKernelCryptography()`/`AddSharedKernelCompression()` called twice register every service exactly once, non-keyed and keyed), T-86 (a consumer fake registered before `AddSharedKernelCryptography()`/`AddSharedKernelCompression()`/`AddSharedKernelFeatureManagement()`/`AddClock()` always wins), T-87 (two distinct `AddNationalIdValidator<T>()` calls both survive in the registry; the same validator registered twice collapses to one), and T-88 (`AddInMemoryLocalizationCatalog()`/`AddStringLocalizerCatalog<TResource>()` now resolve whichever ran FIRST, both call orders proven) all added as new tests. DO-48: XML docs on every converted method state the `TryAdd*`/`TryAddEnumerable` idiom and its override-priority implication; `01.Core/README.md` gained a new "DI Registration Conventions (SK.01.P518)" section, placed immediately after the package table, documenting the domain-wide convention, both named multi-implementation-collection exceptions, and the `SharedKernel.Localization` first-wins inversion. **A second genuine multi-implementation-collection regression, of the identical class D-92 already caught for `INationalIdValidator`, was found and fixed during this implementation pass — not anticipated by the phase's own design or C-110's literal instruction:** `AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptionsValidator`'s own `IValidateOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions>` registration was converted to a plain `TryAddSingleton` per C-110's text, but `IValidateOptions<TOptions>` is ALSO a genuine multi-implementation collection (the options-validation pipeline runs every registered validator for a type) and the immediately preceding `AddValidatedOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions>(section)` call already registers the BCL's own `DataAnnotationValidateOptions<T>` against the identical service type via `ValidateDataAnnotations()` — the plain `TryAddSingleton` therefore silently never registered the custom cross-field validator, confirmed by two host-startup tests (`EmptyKeyNames_ThrowsAtHostStartup`, `CurrentKeyIdNotInKeyNames_ThrowsAtHostStartup`) that had stopped throwing. Fixed to `TryAddEnumerable(ServiceDescriptor.Singleton<IValidateOptions<AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptions>, AzureKeyVaultCryptographyOptionsValidator>())`, matching XML-doc corrections on both the extension method and the validator, and a new regression test (`CalledTwice_CrossFieldValidatorStillEnforced`) proving the fix survives repeated calls — 70/70 `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure.Tests` passing (up from 69/69, two silently broken until this fix). Also fixed, as a separate pre-existing build break discovered and resolved before this phase's own work began: `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation`'s `MustBeValidIban<T>()` failed to compile against P-521's new optional `IbanValidator.Validate(string?, bool)` parameter — an optional parameter still changes method-group-conversion arity — resolved by exposing the matching `allowFallbackForUnknownCountry = false` parameter on the adapter rule itself (default preserves prior behavior exactly), with 3 new regression tests (39/39 `SharedKernel.Validation.FluentValidation.Tests`, up from 36/36). - [2026-09-11] SK.01.P529 opened and taken to 18/19 `●` (user-directed pre-publish hardening of `SharedKernel.Primitives`, the first `01.Core` package slated to publish; only P-51, the publish itself, remains `○`). Six defects found by EXECUTING the compiled assembly and running the trim/AOT analyzers over it — four of which directly contradicted the affected type's own XML docs, so reading would not have found them: `default(Result).Error` returned `null` in violation of `Error`'s own never-null contract (reachable via a failed `TryGetValue` out-param, `new Result[n]`, or an unassigned field — not just a literal `default`); `ValidationResult`/`<T>` equality broken ASYMMETRICALLY (failures unequal, successes equal by accident of sharing one static empty array); `ValidationResult`/`<T>` mutable through the caller's list, letting `live.Clear()` produce a failed result with zero errors — the exact state `Failure`'s own guard rejects; a real `IL2059` emitted into every trimming consumer's build while `PackageTags`/`<Description>` advertised `aot` and the code asserted no annotation was needed; `TryFromValue(null)` throwing instead of returning `false`; and unguarded null on both `Failure` factories. All fixed, each pinned by EXECUTED PERTURBATION (revert → 4/4/2/2 failures respectively). Added `ErrorCodes.Forbidden`, `WellKnownBaggageKeys.TenantId` (value `"TenantId"`, matching the wire — `BaggageLogRecordProcessor` copies baggage generically so the key IS the log property name), `SmartEnum` `IComparable`/`TryFromName`/`SmartEnumJsonConverter`, and `[DebuggerDisplay]` across six types. XML docs now ship for the first time (`GenerateDocumentationFile` must be per-project; the SDK defeats `Directory.Build.targets`' guarded default) and README rewritten 38 → ~250 lines, correcting a "Zero external dependencies" claim that survived P-517 in the README. **Two design decisions that went against the first instinct, both caught by measurement:** the `IComparable` constraint widening compiled here and passed a survey of every concrete subclass (all `int`) but broke `SharedKernel.Core`'s generic `Guard.Against.InvalidSmartEnum<TEnum, TValue>` forwarder with `CS0314` — so comparison uses `Comparer<TValue>.Default` and the constraint is unchanged (lesson: surveying concrete subclasses is not enough, generic forwarders in other packages must be searched too); and the JSON converter's obvious implementation emitted `IL2026`+`IL3050` from `JsonSerializer.Deserialize<T>`/`Serialize`/`GetConverter`, nearly shipping the same defect class being fixed in the same commit — only the `JsonTypeInfo<T>` overloads are unannotated. Verified: 239/239 package tests (was 152), 0 IL warnings under both analyzers, full solution 0 errors, 5,261 tests across 49 projects 0 failures, nupkg ships its `.xml` with one dependency and zero `SharedKernel.*` deps. Declined/deferred: no metadata bag on `Error` (standing ruling); `13.ServiceDefaults`' local `TenantBaggageKeys` not re-pointed (out of scope, now a behaviour-identical refactor); no null guards on `Error.Code`/`Message` (user-directed session)
- [2026-09-11] SK.01.P530 opened and taken to 12/13 `●` (user-directed pre-publish hardening of `SharedKernel.Configuration`, chosen next on measured grounds — 23 referencing projects, the most of any wave-2 package, and the gate for the whole `SharedKernel.Cryptography` sub-tree; only P-56, the publish, remains `○`). Six defects, every one found by EXECUTION or by the trim/AOT analyzers rather than by reading: `AddValidatedOptions<TOptions, TValidator>` used `TryAddSingleton` on `IValidateOptions<TOptions>`, a multi-implementation COLLECTION service, so a caller's validator was silently dropped whenever any other validator for that options type already existed — including the one the sibling overload adds — and configuration violating a cross-property rule (`MinSize=100, MaxSize=1`) started the host cleanly; two distinct validators for one options type likewise lost the second; the package advertised `aot` in `PackageTags` and "AOT-clean" in its README while emitting 6× `IL2091` + 4× `IL2026` + 2× `IL3050` into every trimming consumer's build, because `OptionsBuilder<T>.Bind` is annotated `[RequiresUnreferencedCode]`+`[RequiresDynamicCode]` and BOTH overloads call it — so only *validation*, never *binding*, was ever reflection-free; the DataAnnotations overload was not idempotent, producing 4 duplicated failure messages for 2 broken properties on a second registration; the nuspec forced a DEAD `SharedKernel.Primitives` dependency on every consumer (no `SharedKernel` type appears anywhere in the source); and no `.xml` shipped (same per-project `GenerateDocumentationFile` cause as P-529). Defect 1 is precisely the regression class `01.Core/README.md` already documents as named `TryAdd` exception #2 — `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure` hand-rolls `TryAddEnumerable` next to its own `AddValidatedOptions` call to route around it — so the convention was recorded after P-518 found it downstream while the package that CAUSES it went unfixed; the pre-existing `CalledTwice_RegistersValidatorOnlyOnce` test asserted the broken behaviour as the contract. Added `ISectionBoundOptions` (`static abstract string SectionName`, zero reflection) plus two `IConfiguration` overloads reading it — 33 options types across 12 domains already declare a `SectionName` const and 28 call sites retype `GetSection(X.SectionName)`, a convention root `CLAUDE.md` mandates and nothing enforced; named-options support on all four overloads; and a `validateDataAnnotations` flag that defect 1 had made unusable. **One design decision that went against the first instinct:** the obvious `TryAddEnumerable` fix for the idempotency defect would have introduced a NEW one, since it de-duplicates on implementation type while every named instance shares `DataAnnotationValidateOptions<TOptions>` — which is itself name-scoped and skips other names — leaving every named instance after the first entirely unvalidated; the fix is a per-name check over `ServiceDescriptor.ImplementationInstance`, possible only because the validator is registered as a pre-built immutable instance rather than a factory. Verified: 41/41 package tests (was 10), each fix pinned by executed perturbation (2/2/2/3 failures across four reverts); 12 IL warnings → 0 under both analyzers; full solution 0 errors with no new warning at any of the 27 in-repo `AddValidatedOptions` call sites; 5,310 tests across 49 projects, 0 failures; packed nupkg ships its `.xml` (10 documented members) with zero `SharedKernel.*` dependencies; every README sample compiled and executed. Declined/deferred: `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`'s hand-rolled workaround left as-is (correct, and that package's own scope); the 33 existing options types not retrofitted onto `ISectionBoundOptions` (opt-in, and adoption needs `const string SectionName` → `static string SectionName =>`, since a const field cannot satisfy a `static abstract` property)
- [2026-09-14] SK.01.P538 opened and taken to 11/12 `●` (user-directed pre-first-publish hardening of `SharedKernel.Core`; only P-60, the publish, remains `○`). Ten defects found by executing a probe app: the async railway's `ContinueWith(t => t.Result)` wrapped faulted and cancelled sources in `AggregateException`; functional guards threw on null input; `ToCamelCase("HTMLParser")` returned `hTMLParser`; guard messages used the server culture; `ToUnixMilliseconds` disagreed with the BCL before 1970; `EndOfDay` dropped the last sub-millisecond; `new DomainException(null)` threw `NullReferenceException`; `using SharedKernel.Guards;` did not compile `Guard.Against.*` (extensions lived in `.Clauses`); no XML docs shipped; guard error codes were imprecise. Breaking cleanups taken while unpublished: `.Clauses`/`.Descriptions` folded into `SharedKernel.Guards`, `NotPositive` and five BCL-duplicate helpers deleted, numeric guards generalized to `INumber<T>`. Added: `Ensure`/`TapError`/non-generic railway/`ValueTask` sources/`GetValueOrThrow`, `ResultTry` void and token overloads, `ForbiddenException`, `error.ToException()`, `Guard.Collect`, `ToResult`, four new guards, `PublicApiAnalyzers`. Compiler-found constraint: a source can take `Task` or `ValueTask` continuations, never both, or every async lambda is CS0121-ambiguous. Verified: 317/317 package tests, 10 dependent suites green, 77/77 consumer tests against packed packages, full solution 0 errors, 0 IL warnings, RS0016 proven to fail the build on a missing API line, nupkg ships 243 documented members
